- C-772 **`GET /v1/admin/settings/{section}`** → `SettingsSection { section, generation, updated_at, updated_by_label, settings: Setting[], extra?: object }`; `Setting { key, value, source: "helm" \| "dashboard" \| "default", locked: bool, helm_key, default, list?: "whole" \| "keyed", entries?: [{ id, value, source: "helm" \| "dashboard", locked }], secret?: { set: bool, updated_at: string \| null, from_env?: string } }`. `value` is the effective value; for a secret key it is `null` and only `secret` describes it (`from_env` names the environment variable when the configuration supplies it, which locks it). For a keyed list `value` is the effective list and `entries` carries each entry's source; secret fields inside entries appear as `{ "$secret": { set, updated_at } }`. `extra` is section-specific read-only data (C-778..C-782). 404 `setting_section_unknown`.
- C-773 **`PUT /v1/admin/settings/{section}`** body `{ generation: number, values: { "<key>": value } }`: **replaces the section's dashboard values** — keys absent from `values` return to their default, locked keys must be absent (or equal to the effective value, then ignored). A keyed list (`auth.oidc_providers`, `secrets.stores`, `llm.endpoints` by `id`; `ratelimit.api.classes` by class) is written as the list of its **dashboard** entries only; Helm entries stay as they are and locked. A dashboard entry whose id is a Helm entry's → 422 `settings_invalid` with the field error `{ path: "/<key>/<id>/id", code: "entry_id_in_helm" }` (coordinator review, 2026-11-14). Secret fields: `{ "$secret": "<plaintext>" }` sets a new value, `{ "$secret": "keep" }` keeps the stored one (400 `invalid_request` when there is none), absent or `null` clears it. A secret key that Helm sets is locked like any other (409 `setting_locked` for `set` and `keep`). `keep` on a keyed-list entry whose connection fields changed → 422 `secret_reentry_required` (C-746). All settings writers are serialised by one advisory lock, so the cross-section validation always reads the committed state; secrets replaced by a committed write are discarded after `SECRET_MIGRATION_DISCARD_GRACE`, and secrets sealed for a write that then fails at once. → 200 `SettingsSection`. Errors: 400 `invalid_request` (shape, `details.fields`), 400 `setting_unknown` `{ key }`, 409 `stale_revision` `{ current_generation }`, 409 `setting_locked` `{ key, helm_key }` (a locked leaf key or whole list with a different value), 412 `reauth_required` (session's last OIDC authentication older than 600 s, as `DELETE /v1/me`), 413 `settings_too_large`, 422 `settings_invalid` (`details.fields[]: { path: "/<key>" or "/<key>/<entry id>/<field>", code, message }`; a path may name a key of another section when a cross-section rule fails), 422 `setting_requires_deployment` `{ key, requires }` (C-782), 422 `last_oidc_provider`, 503 `audit_unavailable`. Effects: one transaction (row CAS, sealing new secrets beforehand and discarding replaced ones after commit, critical audit C-777), then `NOTIFY aurora_settings`, then the e-mails of C-777. `write` rate class.
- C-774 **`POST /v1/admin/settings/{section}/preview`**: the PUT body, nothing written, no re-authentication → `{ generation, diff: [{ key, entry_id?, before, after, before_source, after_source }], loosening: [{ key, entry_id?, reason }], affected_orgs: [{ org_id, slug, name, changes: [{ key, before, after }] }], affected_orgs_total, warnings: [{ code, key?, message }] }`. Secret values appear as `"(secret)"`. `affected_orgs` lists organizations whose effective values change (sections with org layering: `governance`, `llm`, `retention`, `scim`; empty elsewhere), at most `SETTINGS_PREVIEW_ORGS_MAX`. The same 400/409/413/422 errors as PUT. Warning codes are an open vocabulary (`removes_own_provider`, `grants_instance_admin`, `opens_private_destinations`, `secrets_become_non_permitted`, `identities_orphaned`, …); clients show unknown codes by `message`.
- C-775 **`GET /v1/admin/settings/export?format=yaml|json&sections=a,b`** → the document `{ aurora_settings: 1, exported_at, sections: { "<section>": { "<key>": value } }, locked: [{ section, key, helm_key }] }` as `application/yaml` (default) or `application/json`, `content-disposition: attachment; filename="aurora-settings-<UTC date>.<ext>"`. Only `dashboard` values; secret fields as `{ "$secret": "set" }` (or absent when unset); `locked` is informational. `export` rate class. Not audited (no secret values leave).
- C-776 **`POST /v1/admin/settings/import`** body `{ document: string, format: "yaml" \| "json", dry_run: bool, mode?: "replace" \| "merge", generations?: { "<section>": number } }`. A document is parsed with the C-775 shape (unknown `aurora_settings` version → 400). `replace` (default): each section in the document becomes exactly its listed values (as C-773); `merge`: only the listed keys change. A locked key equal to its effective value is ignored (warning `locked_ignored`), any other → 409 `setting_locked`. `{ "$secret": "set" }` keeps the stored secret (422 `settings_invalid` with the field error code `secret_missing` when none is stored; for a secret key that Helm sets it is ignored with `locked_ignored`, anything else → 409 `setting_locked`); a plaintext `$secret` in an import → 400 `secret_in_import` (secrets are entered on the page). Dry run → `{ sections: [{ section, generation, diff, loosening, warnings }], errors: [...] }` (C-774 shapes; no re-authentication). Apply (`dry_run: false`) requires `generations` for every section the document touches (409 `stale_revision` `{ section, current_generation }`), re-authentication (412), and applies all sections in **one transaction** (all or nothing), one critical audit row `instance_settings.import`, one notify per section, one e-mail. Body > `SETTINGS_IMPORT_BYTES_MAX` → 413. `write` rate class.
- C-777 **Audit and notification.** `instance_settings.update` / `.import` / `.reset` (CLI): security-critical, `org_id` null, `target_kind` `instance_settings`, `target_id` the section (import: `null` with `metadata.sections`), `source` `dashboard` / `api` / `cli`, `metadata`: `{ section, generation, change: [{ key, entry_id?, before, after }] }` (secret fields `"(secret changed)"` / `"(secret removed)"`, values cut at `SETTINGS_AUDIT_VALUE_MAX`). `instance_settings.helm_sync`: best-effort system row `{ section, keys }`. After commit every instance admin who is not disabled gets an e-mail "Aurora: instance settings changed (<section titles>)": actor, time, the changed key names (never values), a link to `/admin/audit?action=instance_settings`; log-only mode logs it like other mail.
- C-778 **Governance** (SET-2). Keys as `15 §Instance settings` with the types and rules of `08 §11` and `15 §Configuration reference`. `extra`: `{ orgs_url }`. **`GET /v1/admin/settings/governance/orgs?limit=&cursor=`** → `Page<{ org_id, slug, name, frozen, llm_posture, egress_posture, open_lane, byo_posture, stricter: [key], clamped: [key] }>` — effective values only, no content (`stricter`: org settings tighter than the instance floor; `clamped`: org settings currently overridden by it). The preview's `affected_orgs` uses the same computation. A saved change enqueues the `08 §11.2` re-evaluation for every organization (idempotent job; progress is not exposed). The page states that policy is set per organization and workspace (no instance rule scope, D-195(f)).
- C-779 **LLM endpoints** (SET-3). `llm.endpoints` is a **keyed list by endpoint `id`** (coordinator review, 2026-11-14): GET returns the effective list with `entries[]` carrying each endpoint's source (`helm` entries locked, "Managed by Helm"); PUT sends only the dashboard endpoints, and one reusing a Helm endpoint's `id` → 422 `entry_id_in_helm` on `/llm.endpoints/<id>/id` (C-773). `llm.enabled_providers` and `llm.provider_preference` are whole values (locked as a whole when Helm sets them). Entries have the TOML shape of `15` as JSON (credential-like keys refused as at startup); endpoint ids are unique across both sources, so LLM secrets' `llm_endpoint_id` keeps naming exactly one endpoint. Removing or narrowing an endpoint that LLM secrets use is allowed; the preview warns `secrets_become_non_permitted` with counts per org. **`POST /v1/admin/settings/llm/endpoint-test`** body `{ endpoint: <entry> }` → `{ reachable: bool, status?: number, error_code?: "destination_refused" \| "dns" \| "timeout" \| "connect" \| "tls" \| "unexpected_status" \| "unreachable", models?: string[] }`: one unauthenticated request from the API through the destination guard (`gateway.allowed_private` applies) to the endpoint's models path, 10 s; never a credential; `models` only when the endpoint answers without one.
- C-780 **Sign-in** (SET-4). `auth.oidc_providers` entries: the TOML provider shape as JSON with the secret field `client_secret` (C-773 `$secret`) instead of `client_secret_env`; a mirrored entry that carries `client_secret_env` keeps it (shown as `secret.from_env`) until a secret is set. `id` is immutable for dashboard entries (changing it = remove + add; the preview warns `identities_orphaned` with the count). Saving validates issuer discovery (422 `settings_invalid` code `oidc_discovery_failed` with `reason`). **`POST /v1/admin/settings/sign_in/provider-test`** body `{ provider: <entry without secret> }` → `{ ok: bool, issuer?, authorization_endpoint?, token_endpoint?, jwks_uri?, error_code? }`. `extra`: `{ providers_from_helm: n, signed_in_with: <provider id> }`. Preview warnings `grants_instance_admin` (a mapping to `instance_admin` added or widened) and `removes_own_provider`.
- C-781 **Email** (SET-4). Keys `email.smtp_url`, `email.from`, `email.smtp_username`, `email.smtp_password` (secret; locked when `AURORA_SMTP_PASSWORD` is set). **`POST /v1/admin/settings/email/test`** body `{ values?: <section values as C-773> }` (absent = effective settings) → `{ sent: bool, error_code?: "connect" \| "tls" \| "auth" \| "rejected" \| "timeout" \| "log_only" }`, sent to the caller's own address only; `export` rate class.
- C-782 **Operational sections** (SET-5). `agents.extra` = `{ runners: [{ id, name, sandbox_memory_mib, sandbox_cpus, sandbox_pids, home_gib, max_sandboxes, harnesses: [string] }] }`, read-only from runner registrations (D-235(a)); `hosted.enabled_harnesses` outside the union of `harnesses` → 422 `settings_invalid` code `harness_not_available`. `terminal.extra` / `scim.extra` = `{ available: bool }`; turning `terminal.enabled` / `scim.enabled` on while `available` is false → 422 `setting_requires_deployment` `{ key, requires: "terminal.available" \| "scim.available", helm_key: "terminal.enabled" \| "scim.enabled" }`. `limits`: `ratelimit.api.classes` is keyed by class name (entries `{ class, per_minute?, per_hour?, burst }`); a change adding an `allowed_private` entry is reported as loosening with warning `opens_private_destinations`.
- C-783 **"Enforced by instance" names its setting.** Org-level responses that report an instance clamp (`GET /v1/orgs/{org}/governance/effective`: each value with `source: instance`, and `GET /v1/governance/instance`, BYO posture refusals' `details`, retention and attachment ceilings, SCIM domain refusals) gain `instance_setting: { section, key }` beside the existing fields (additive). The dashboard links it for instance admins only.
- C-784 **CLI** (`aurora-api settings …`, operator contract, D-238(b)): `show [<section>] [--json]` prints effective values with sources (secrets as set/unset); `reset <section> [--key <key>] [--entry <id>] [--dry-run]` removes dashboard values (whole section, one key, or one keyed entry) through the settings service (validation of the result, generation bump, `instance_settings.reset` critical audit with source `cli` and actor label `cli`, notify, e-mail); `export [--format yaml|json] [--sections …]` writes C-775 to stdout; `import --file <f> [--dry-run] [--mode replace|merge]` follows C-776 without re-authentication. Exit codes: 0 done, 2 validation refused (message names the key), 3 locked key, 4 database unreachable. Starts with invalid stored settings (it loads the configuration without them).
- C-746 **Secret stores** (FIX-124, lane `p5-apps`; every write is C-773 on the `secrets` section, so locks, `stale_revision`, 412 re-authentication, `settings_invalid`, the critical `instance_settings.update` audit with its diff, the admin e-mail and the `aurora_settings` reload apply unchanged; IA). `GET /v1/admin/secret-stores` → `{ default_store: { value, source }, effective_default, generation, stores: [{ id, kind, source: builtin|helm|dashboard, helm_key?, settings (no credential; a sealed one as `{ $secret: { set: true } }`), references, default, health: { ok, checked_at, error_code?: unavailable|init_failed }, error?, managed_values, reference_values, reference_scopes: [{ org_id, org_slug?, paths }] }], migration }`; health and the counts (read from the `SecretRef` columns) are probed at most once per `SECRET_STORE_HEALTH_INTERVAL`. `PUT /v1/admin/secret-stores/{id}` `{ generation, store }` adds or replaces a dashboard store (`kind` `hashicorp-vault` only — 422 `store_kind_not_allowed`; a Helm store's id → 422 `entry_id_in_helm`); `store` is `{ kind, address, kv_mount?, managed_prefix?, auth: { method: approle, mount?, role_id, secret_id }, ca_pem?, reference_scopes? }` (a dashboard store is AppRole only, D-307: `auth.method = "kubernetes"` → 422 `dashboard_reference_refused` on `/secrets.stores/<id>/auth/method`; Helm stores keep `kubernetes`) and the AppRole secret id is `{ $secret: "<value>" }` (sealed in the **inline** store, purpose `instance_setting`) or `{ $secret: "keep" }`. `DELETE /v1/admin/secret-stores/{id}?generation=` (409 `store_not_empty` `{ managed_values, reference_values }`, `store_is_default`, `setting_locked` for a Helm store). `PUT /v1/admin/secret-store-default` `{ generation, store_id }` (422 `store_not_usable` for a store that is missing, failed to start or is not managed). `PUT /v1/admin/secret-stores/{id}/reference-scopes` `{ generation, scopes: [{ org_id, paths }] }` (SEC-61 globs validated by `VaultSettings`; 422 `org_not_found`). `POST /v1/admin/secret-store-test` `{ id, store }` → `{ ok, steps: [{ name: configuration\|authenticate\|write\|read\|delete, ok, error_code? }], checked_at }`: builds the store from the definition and writes, reads and removes a probe value; nothing is saved; `{ $secret: "keep" }` uses the stored value of the same id. **Dashboard stores are narrower than Helm ones (SEC-89, SEC-90; review 2026-11-15):** a dashboard store may not carry `ca_file`, `auth.role_id_env`, `auth.secret_id_env`, `auth.token`, `auth.token_env` or `auth.jwt_path` (an environment variable or file of the server must never be sent to an admin-chosen host): 422 `settings_invalid` with the field error `{ path: "/secrets.stores/<id>/auth/<field>", code: "dashboard_reference_refused" }` (credentials are a literal `role_id` + sealed `secret_id`, the CA is `ca_pem`; those fields stay available to Helm stores). A dashboard store's `address` that is a cloud-metadata address, or that resolves to a private, loopback or link-local one not opened by `gateway.allowed_private`, is refused with the field error code `destination_refused` (names that do not resolve at save time are accepted; the store's HTTP client judges every connect again). `{ $secret: "keep" }` is refused with 422 `secret_reentry_required` (`details.fields[0].path` names the store) when any other field of the entry except `reference_scopes`, `references`, `kv_mount` and `managed_prefix` differs from the stored entry: a stored secret is never carried to another address, mount, role or CA (applies to every keyed-list entry of C-773, and to `secret-store-test`, which answers `{ ok: false, steps: [{ name: "configuration", error_code: "secret_reentry_required" \| "dashboard_reference_refused:<field>" \| "destination_refused" }] }`).
- C-747 **Secret migration** (FIX-124). `POST /v1/admin/secret-stores/migrations` `{ target, dry_run? }`: a dry run answers 200 `{ state: "dry_run", per_purpose: [{ purpose, moved (would move), already, … }] }` and writes nothing; a real run needs re-authentication (412), answers 202 and runs in the background (the re-seal of `aurora-api secrets migrate`: read → put → compare-and-swap → discard the old value; idempotent, resumable by running it again); one run at a time across replicas **and the CLI** (a transaction-scoped advisory lock: 409 `migration_running`; the CLI says "another secrets migration is running"). `GET /v1/admin/secret-stores/migrations/current` → `{ state: none\|running\|succeeded\|failed\|dry_run, target, started_at, finished_at, started_by, per_purpose: [{ purpose, moved, skipped, failed, already, total }], error?, running_elsewhere }` (progress is the replica's own; another replica or the CLI shows `running_elsewhere`; a finished run is read from its audit row). Audit `secret_stores.migrate_start` and `secret_stores.migrate_finish` (security-critical; the finish carries the report). The instance settings' sealed fields (a Vault AppRole secret id among them) are never moved to another store by the dashboard job or the CLI (D-236(g)); `--to inline` still re-seals them.
- C-748 **Live store registry** (FIX-124). The API and the gateway rebuild their store registry when `secrets.stores` or `secrets.default_store` changes (settings reload, by notify, poll or reconnect), swapping it atomically: a call in flight finishes on the registry it started with. A store that fails to initialise stays listed with its error (`error_code: init_failed`) and is never the default (the effective default falls back to `inline` and `effective_default` says so). The gateway loads the stored settings before serving. `aurora-api secrets migrate|rekey` load them too, so `--to` accepts a dashboard store. A dashboard store's address is judged again whenever the registry is built (IP literals, which the HTTP client does not resolve) and by the client's resolver at every connect, so removing a range from `gateway.allowed_private` revokes it; the save-time DNS check runs before the settings write lock is taken and is bounded by the store probe timeout; a dashboard store that fails the check stays listed with `init_failed`. The same refusals (cross rules on reload) apply to stored entries. When the configured default cannot be built the API logs it at error level and `effective_default` (`inline`) differs from `default_store.value`; the overview reads that.
- C-745 **Settings, additive to C-772** (SET-1, lane `p5-apps`; `docs/tasks/SET-1.md`). Each `Setting` also carries `schema: { kind: "bool" | "int" | "duration_secs" | "string" | "enum" | "list" | "object", min?, max?, max_len?, options?, item_kind? }` (the registry's type, so a generic form renders and bounds a control without knowing the key) and, for a keyed list, `id_field` (`id`, or `class`). `GET /v1/admin/settings/{section}` for a section whose keys SET-2..SET-5 have not registered answers an empty `settings` list. The audit action `instance_settings.update` metadata is `{ section, generation, change: [{ key, entry_id?, before, after }] }` as C-777. The settings export is in the `export` rate class (C-775).

### Phase 5 — P4-SSH (SSH access to agent sandboxes, D-239)

Rule: `04 §SSH access`, `05 §SSH access` and `§Terminator plane`, `11 §SSH access`, `12 §SSH channels`, `16 §W20`, `03 §Planned changes`, `14`, `15 §SSH access`, `23`, `02 §Limits`, SEC-92..SEC-96. Reserved numbers C-790..C-799. Errors use the standard body. Off unless `ssh.enabled`: every route below answers 404 `ssh_disabled` while it is off. P4-SSH-1 implements C-790, C-791, C-792, C-795 (keys part), C-798; P4-SSH-2 the rest.

- C-790 **SSH keys** (`/v1/me/ssh-keys`): `SshKey = { id, name, algorithm: "ssh-ed25519", public_key, fingerprint, created_at, last_used_at: string|null }`. `GET` (S/K) → `{ keys: SshKey[] }` (newest first). `POST` (SO) `{ public_key: string ≤ 4096, name?: string ≤ 80 }` → 201 `SshKey`: the line is parsed with the `ssh-key` crate; accepted only a single `ssh-ed25519 <base64> [comment]` line (no options prefix, no certificate, no private key, no other algorithm); stored canonical (`ssh-ed25519 <base64>`), `fingerprint` = `SHA256:` + unpadded base64 of SHA-256 of the key blob (equals `ssh-keygen -lf`), `name` = given name, else the comment (sanitized, ≤ 80), else "SSH key". Errors 422 `ssh_key_invalid`, 422 `ssh_key_unsupported_type`, 409 `ssh_key_exists` (same fingerprint for this person), 409 `ssh_key_limit` (10). `PATCH /{id}` (SO) `{ name }` → `SshKey`; `DELETE /{id}` (SO) → 204; another person's id → 404. API keys on the write routes → 403 `session_required`. Audit `ssh_key.create` / `ssh_key.delete` (security-critical, metadata name + fingerprint).
- C-791 **Certificate mint** `POST /v1/agents/{agent}/ssh-certificate` (S, or K of the caller: `auk_` only for agents of its workspace, `auo_` per its owner's role; service keys and agent tokens 403): body `{ public_key }`; the key's fingerprint must be one of the caller's `ssh_keys` (422 `ssh_key_not_registered`). `can_ssh` (`04 §SSH access`): unknown or invisible agent 404, BYO 422 `ssh_not_supported`, frozen 423 `frozen`. Counter: `ssh_mint_counters` (person, agent, hour) in the mint transaction, > 30 → 429 `ssh_mint_rate_limited` with `Retry-After` (seconds to the hour's end). Response 201 `{ certificate: string, serial: string (u64 decimal), principal: string (agent id), key_id: string, valid_after: RFC3339, valid_before: RFC3339, username: string (agent id), host: string, port: int, known_hosts: string[] }`. Certificate: `ssh-ed25519-cert-v01@openssh.com`, type user, random non-zero serial, `key_id = "aurora:u=<user>:k=<ssh key id>:a=<agent>:w=<workspace>"`, principals `[agent id]`, `valid_after = now − 60 s`, `valid_before = now + ssh.cert_ttl_secs`, critical options none, extensions exactly `permit-pty`, signed by the `local` signer's key (`AURORA_SSH_CA_KEY`); the line's comment is `aurora:<agent id>`. Stamps `ssh_keys.last_used_at`; audit `agent.ssh_certificate.mint` (agent, workspace, fingerprint, serial, `valid_before`). Signer seam: `trait SshCaSigner: Send + Sync { fn public_key(&self) -> ssh_key::PublicKey; async fn sign(&self, tbs: CertificateBuilder) -> Result<Certificate> }` in the API, chosen by config (`local` only in this batch).
- C-792 **Connection facts** `GET /v1/ssh` (S/K): `{ host, port, cert_ttl_secs, terminators_live: int, host_keys: [{ public_key, fingerprint }], known_hosts: string[] }` — `host`/`port` from `ssh.public_host`/`ssh.public_port`; host keys are the distinct `host_public_key` values of replicas with `last_seen_at > now − 90 s` (empty before P4-SSH-2); a `known_hosts` line is `<host> <key>` when the port is 22, else `[<host>]:<port> <key>`. `GET /v1/instance` gains `features.ssh: bool`. `GET /v1/agents/{agent}/sandbox`'s `keep_awake_reasons` gains `"ssh"` (P4-SSH-2).
- C-793 **Sessions** (P4-SSH-2): `SshSession = { id, agent: { id, name }, workspace_id, user?: { id, label }, key: { id: string|null, name: string|null, fingerprint }, client_address: string|null, opened_at, last_activity_at: string|null, channels_open: int, channels_total: int }`. `GET /v1/me/ssh-sessions` (S/K) → the caller's open sessions; `GET /v1/agents/{agent}/ssh-sessions` (S/K) → open sessions on the agent (`can_own`: all, with `user`; `can_use`: own only; otherwise 404); `DELETE /v1/ssh-sessions/{id}` (S/K) → 202 `{ disconnecting: true }` for the session's person or `can_own` (else 404 `ssh_session_not_found`), sets `revoke_requested_at`/`revoke_reason` (`user_disconnect`|`owner_disconnect`), audit `ssh_session.disconnect`. `GET /v1/admin/terminators` (IA) → `{ terminators: [{ id, version, host_key_fingerprint, sessions_open, last_seen_at, live }] }`.
- C-794 **Terminator plane** `/v1/terminator/*` (`aut_` bearer, constant-time hash compare against `AURORA_BOOTSTRAP_TERMINATOR_TOKEN`, 401 uniform; `X-Aurora-Replica` after register; rate class `SSH_API_RATE` per replica; types in `aurora-protocol::terminator`): `register { version, host_public_key }` → `{ replica_id, protocol: 1, config: TerminatorConfig }`, `TerminatorConfig = { ca_public_keys: string[], heartbeat_secs: 30, lease_secs: 90, wake_wait_secs, idle_timeout_secs, max_lifetime_secs, channels_per_session: 4 }`; `sessions { certificate, username, client_address }` → 201 `{ session_id, agent_id, sandbox: "running"|"waking"|"stopped", lease_secs, expires_at }` | 403 `{ reason: "denied"|"limit" }` (the API logs the precise cause at debug, never the certificate); `sessions/{id}/channels { mode, wait_secs? ≤ 45 }` → `{ grant: "auz_…", expires_in: 30, runner_endpoint }` | 403 `session_closed` | 409 `not_running { retry_after_secs }` | 429 `ssh_channel_limit` | 429 `wake_limited`; `heartbeat { sessions: [{ id, last_activity_at?, channels_open, channels_total, bytes_in, bytes_out }] ≤ 500 }` → `{ revoked: [{ id, reason }], config: TerminatorConfig }`; `sessions/{id}/close { reason, channels_total, channels_by_mode: { shell, exec, sftp }, bytes_in, bytes_out }` → `{ ok: true }` (idempotent). Session open is one transaction: certificate re-verification, key lookup by `(user_id, fingerprint)` and key id match, `can_ssh`, caps over open lease-current rows (`FOR UPDATE` on the user's and agent's open rows), insert, wake demand, `last_used_at`; the audit `agent.ssh.open` is best-effort after commit. Sweep: every 30 s per replica, `SKIP LOCKED`.
- C-795 **Dashboard** (`14`): Account › SSH keys (`/account/ssh`; P4-SSH-1 the keys table and add/rename/delete; P4-SSH-2 adds Active SSH sessions) and the agent page's **SSH** page (`?tab=ssh`, Computer section; P4-SSH-1 the Connect-with-SSH card, certificate download, host-key card — empty-state notice until a terminator registers — and "What works"; P4-SSH-2 the sessions list and the status-rail link). Shared primitives only (`Card`, `CodeBlock`, `ResourceTable`, `Dialog`, `ConfirmDialog`, `Notice`, `Select`), both themes, no horizontal scroll at 390 px. Admin › Overview SSH line (P4-SSH-2).
- C-796 **Wire** (`16 §W20`, P4-SSH-2): leg 1 `GET /v1/preview-stream` with `Bearer auz_…`; runner redemption `POST /v1/runner/preview-streams/open { kind: "ssh", grant }` → `{ sandbox_id, boot_id, window, idle_secs, max_lifetime_secs }`; first Text frame `exec.open` (≤ 12 KiB, within 5 s) → `exec.opened` | close 4502/4409; leg-1 frames: Binary `[0x02|0x03][payload]`, Text `pty.resize`, `exec.eof`, `exec.exit`; leg 2 `exec.open { pty_id, window, mode, command?, tty?, env }` → `exec.opened { pty_id, window }` | `exec.refused { pty_id, reason: "disabled"|"limit"|"unsupported"|"error" }`, `exec.eof { pty_id }`, `exec.exit { pty_id, status?, signal? }`, `pty.resize`/`pty.credit`/`pty.close` as W19, data `[0x02|0x03][pty_id u32 BE][≤ 16 KiB]`; runner keep-awake `POST /v1/runner/terminal/keep-awake { sandbox_id, kind?: "terminal"|"ssh" }` (absent = terminal) → `ssh_active_until` (`SSH_AWAKE_GRACE`, cap `SSH_AWAKE_CAP` via `ssh_awake_since`); runner capability `ssh` registered when the API's registration config says `ssh_enabled: true`.
- C-797 **Config, Helm, NetworkPolicy** (`15 §SSH access`, `23` planned values; P4-SSH-1 the `ssh.*` API config keys; P4-SSH-2 the rest): config keys `ssh.enabled`, `ssh.public_host`, `ssh.public_port`, `ssh.cert_ttl_secs`, `ssh.ca.previous_public_keys`, `ssh.sessions_per_user`, `ssh.sessions_per_agent`, `ssh.idle_timeout_secs`, `ssh.awake_cap_secs`, `ssh.wake_wait_secs`, `terminator.api_url`, `terminator.listen`, `terminator.ops_port`, `terminator.max_preauth`, `terminator.max_preauth_per_ip`; secrets `AURORA_TERMINATOR_TOKEN`, `AURORA_SSH_CA_KEY` (api only), `AURORA_SSH_HOST_KEY` (terminator only) — formats `ed25519:<base64 32-byte seed>` or unencrypted OpenSSH; startup refuses `ssh.enabled` without the key the service needs. Chart: `ssh.enabled` (mirrors `config.ssh.enabled`), `terminator.*` workload and Service values, `networkPolicy.sshFrom` (required), flows terminator ← sshFrom :2222, terminator → api:4101, runner:4108, DNS only; api ← terminator :4101; runner ← terminator :4108; sandboxes unchanged; image `aurora-terminator`; kind NodePort 2222.
- C-798 **CLI** (`aurora ssh`, P4-SSH-1): `aurora ssh <agent> [--key <path to .pub>] [-- <ssh args…>]` and `aurora ssh cert <agent> [--key …]`; agent = id or handle in the CLI's workspace; the key defaults to `~/.ssh/id_ed25519.pub` and must be registered (else the message names Account › SSH keys); certificate → `~/.config/aurora/ssh/<agent id>-cert.pub` (0600, reused while > 60 s valid), `known_hosts` from `GET /v1/ssh` → `~/.config/aurora/ssh/known_hosts`; `exec` of `ssh` with `-i <private key path> -o CertificateFile=… -o UserKnownHostsFile=… -o StrictHostKeyChecking=yes -o IdentitiesOnly=yes -p <port> <agent id>@<host>`; the CLI never reads the private key file. `ssh_disabled` → "SSH is not enabled on this Aurora instance".
- C-799 **Tests** (`18 §SSH access`): per SEC-92..SEC-96 "Proven by"; the kind e2e `scripts/e2e/ssh.sh` (P4-SSH-2) covers shell, exit status, `sftp` and `scp` round trips, refused forwarding, host-key pinning, wake on connect and freeze revocation.

### Phase 5 — P4-LLM (additional LLM providers, D-121, D-260..D-262)

Rule: `08 §Phase 4 providers` (incl. *Minted credentials*, *Wire formats and harness reach*, *Endpoint destinations*), `08 §LLM secrets`, `06` steps 2, 6a, 11 and §Guidance responses / §Caches, `12 §Harness selection` and the adapters, `14`, `15 §Configuration reference` and `§Instance settings` (`llm`), `23`, `02 §Limits`, `03 §Planned changes`, SEC-64, SEC-65, SEC-97, SEC-98. Reserved numbers C-800..C-819 (C-815..C-819 unused). Migrations `20261201000210..219` reserved, none needed. Not built yet (briefs P4-LLM-1..6). Errors use the standard body. Nothing changes for a deployment that keeps `llm.enabled_providers` at its default.

- C-800 **Endpoint entries per provider** (TOML under `[[llm.endpoints]]` and the same object as JSON in `llm.endpoints` of C-779). Common keys as today (`id`, `provider`, `display_name`, `dialects`, `default_model`, `models`, `allowed_models`, `key_origin`, `allowed_paths`). Provider keys: `openai` — `organization?`, `project?` (strings ≤ 128), no `base_url`, dialects ⊆ `openai_chat`, `openai_responses` (default both); `azure_openai` — `base_url` (required, `https://<host>` with no path, query or userinfo), `models` and `default_model` required, dialects ⊆ `openai_chat`, `openai_responses`; `bedrock` — `region` (required, `^[a-z]{2}(-gov)?-[a-z]+-[0-9]$`; partitions `aws`, `aws-us-gov`), `base_url?` (https VPC-endpoint host, no path), dialects ⊆ `anthropic_bedrock`, `openai_chat`, `openai_responses` (default `["anthropic_bedrock"]`), **no `allowed_paths`**; `vertex` — `project_id` (`^[a-z][a-z0-9-]{4,28}[a-z0-9]$`), `location` (`global` or `^[a-z]+-[a-z]+[0-9]+$`), `models` and `default_model` required, dialects ⊆ `anthropic_vertex`, `openai_chat` (default `["anthropic_vertex"]`), **no `allowed_paths`**, no `base_url`; `openai_compatible` — `base_url` (required; `https`, or `http` per D-262(b)), `auth_header` (`authorization` \| `api-key` \| `x-api-key`), `models_path?` (absolute, ≤ 256), dialects any of the three (default `["openai_chat"]`). Key-origin methods per provider: `openai` `provider_account`; `azure_openai` `endpoint`, `provider_account`; `bedrock` `endpoint`, `provider_account`; `vertex` `provider_account`; `openai_compatible` `endpoint`. Refusals (startup message naming the key; on a settings write 422 `settings_invalid` with `details.fields[] = { path: "/llm.endpoints/<id>/<key>", code }`): `credential_in_entry` (any key named `api_key`, `access_key*`, `secret*`, `private_key`, `password`, `token`, `client_secret`), `key_unknown`, `base_url_invalid`, `base_url_not_allowed` (fixed-host provider), `http_needs_private_host` (dashboard entry), `region_invalid`, `location_invalid`, `project_id_invalid`, `models_required`, `default_model_required`, `dialect_unsupported`, `key_origin_method_unsupported`, `allowed_paths_invalid` (shape, > 32), `allowed_paths_not_supported` (`bedrock`, `vertex`), `value_invalid` (a malformed `organization`, `project`, `auth_header` or `models_path`; D-248(c)), `endpoint_host_claimed` (dashboard entry; `details.fields[].app_id`), plus C-773's `entry_id_in_helm`. One validation function serves start and every write.
- C-801 **LLM secret values per auth mode** (create body `value`, `PUT /v1/secrets/{id}/value`; `llm.auth_mode` must be one the endpoint admits, C-802). `api_key`: string. `aws_sigv4`: `{ access_key_id, secret_access_key, session_token? }`. `aws_assume_role`: `{ access_key_id, secret_access_key, role_arn, session_duration_secs? }` (`role_arn` `arn:aws(-us-gov)?:iam::[0-9]{12}:role/…` whose partition matches the endpoint region; 900–43 200, default 3 600). `azure_entra`: `{ tenant_id, client_id, client_secret }` (GUIDs; `common`/`organizations`/`consumers` refused). `gcp_service_account`: the key file JSON (`type = service_account`, `token_uri = https://oauth2.googleapis.com/token`). Unknown fields are refused (a `token_uri`-, `authority`- or `region`-like field in an AWS or Entra value is `unknown_field`). Errors 422 `llm_key_invalid` with `details.reason` ∈ `auth_mode_unsupported`, `missing_field`, `unknown_field`, `role_arn_invalid`, `partition_mismatch`, `duration_out_of_range`, `tenant_not_specific`, `token_uri_not_google`, `not_service_account`, `assume_role_denied`, `mint_rejected`, `provider_rejected`; unreachable as today (`llm_endpoint_unreachable` with a key-origin rule, else saved `pending`). Responses never echo any value field; `GET /v1/secrets/{id}` shows `llm.auth_mode` and the verified `origin`.
- C-802 **`GET /v1/llm/providers`** (additive): each provider keeps `auth_modes: string[]` (the mode ids, so existing clients keep working; D-248(a)) and gains `auth_mode_details[]` items `{ id, value_shape: "string" | "aws_sigv4" | "aws_assume_role" | "azure_entra" | "gcp_service_account", recommended: bool }` (`aws_assume_role` recommended for `bedrock`); each endpoint gains `auth_modes: string[]` (the modes its key-origin rule admits: an `azure_openai` endpoint with `provider_account` admits `azure_entra` only, a `bedrock` one `aws_assume_role` and `aws_sigv4` only), `recommended_auth_mode`, and `harnesses: string[]` (enabled harnesses whose dialects intersect the endpoint's, D-260(a)). The org's external id is not here (it is `Org.external_id`).
- C-803 **Endpoint test per provider** (extends C-779): the probe is `GET` on `openai` `https://api.openai.com/v1/models`, `azure_openai` `<base>/openai/v1/models`, `bedrock` `<runtime base>/`, `vertex` `https://<host>/v1/projects/<project_id>/locations/<location>`, `openai_compatible` `<base>` + (`models_path` or `/v1/models`), `anthropic` `/v1/models`, `litellm` unchanged; any status < 500 is `reachable: true` with `status`; `models` only when the answer is a 2xx OpenAI-style list (compatible servers without auth). Destination rules of D-262 apply to the probe (a dashboard entry on an uncovered private address → `destination_refused`).
- C-804 **Key origin per provider and mode** (`secrets.origin` keeps its shape): `openai` `organization_id` ← `openai-organization`, `workspace_id` ← `openai-project` response headers (also the response backstop); `azure_openai` + `azure_entra` `organization_id` ← token `tid`, `user_id` ← `appid` (or `azp`); `bedrock` + `aws_sigv4` `organization_id` ← `GetCallerIdentity` account; `bedrock` + `aws_assume_role` `organization_id` ← the account of `AssumedRoleUser.Arn`, `user_id` ← `role_arn`; `vertex` `workspace_id` ← `project_id`, `user_id` ← `client_email`. A mode that cannot report the account under a `provider_account` rule (`api_key` on `azure_openai`/`bedrock`) → 422 `llm_key_unverifiable`. Only `openai` has a response backstop.
- C-805 **Inference path refusals**: 403 `llm_path_not_permitted` `details: { provider, endpoint_id }` (sets in `08`, now including `anthropic`); 413 `llm_request_too_large` `details: { limit_bytes }` for Bedrock `aws_sigv4` and `aws_assume_role`. Both before policy, before any value is opened or minted.
- C-806 **Injection per auth mode** (gateway, step 11; every listed agent header removed first, never merged): `openai` `api_key` → `Authorization: Bearer`, `OpenAI-Organization`/`OpenAI-Project` set from the entry (removed when absent); `azure_openai` `api_key` → `api-key`; `azure_entra` → `Authorization: Bearer <Entra token>` (`api-key` removed); `bedrock` `aws_sigv4`/`aws_assume_role` → SigV4 (`bedrock`, endpoint region; `X-Amz-Security-Token` when a session token exists), all agent `Authorization` and `X-Amz-*` removed; `bedrock` `api_key` → `Authorization: Bearer`; `vertex` → `Authorization: Bearer <Google token>`, agent `x-goog-*` removed; `openai_compatible` → the entry's `auth_header`; `anthropic` unchanged (`x-api-key`). Removed in every case: `authorization`, `x-api-key`, `api-key`, `x-amz-*`, `x-goog-*` (except the gateway's own), `openai-organization`, `openai-project`.
- C-807 **Mint service**: `aurora-llm::mint` holds per minted mode a pinned endpoint, a request builder (`build(endpoint, value, org_external_id?, org_id) -> http::Request<Bytes>`) and a parser (`parse(status, headers, body) -> Result<Minted { material, expires_at, origin }, MintError { class: Rejected | Unavailable, provider_code? }>`), shared by the API (validation) and the gateway. The gateway service (`LlmMint`) caches by `(secret_id, value_version, mode, region)` per `08 §Phase 4 providers` *Minted credentials*; failure → 502 `credential_unavailable` `details: { kind: "llm_mint", reason: "mint_rejected" | "mint_unavailable" }`, request-log `error_code` `llm_auth` (rejected) or `credential_unavailable` (unavailable). Metric `aurora_gateway_llm_mint_total{mode, outcome = "minted" | "cached" | "rejected" | "unavailable" | "held"}`.
- C-808 **Error classification**: `LlmErrorClass` = `auth | rate_limited | quota | overloaded | refusal | other`; `aurora_llm::classify(provider, status, error_type: Option<&str>)` is the one table of `08`; the gateway passes `error_type` only from Bedrock's `x-amzn-ErrorType` header (D-260(d)), adapters pass the body's error type or the stream event's. Request-log `error_code` for an upstream non-2xx on an injected LLM request: `llm_auth`, `llm_rate_limited`, `llm_quota`, `llm_overloaded`, `llm_refusal` (none for `other`); metric `aurora_gateway_llm_upstream_total{provider, endpoint, class}` with `class` additionally `ok` for 2xx. Status and body reach the client unchanged.
- C-809 **`ModelChoice.client_env` per provider and dialect** (placeholders and switches only; identical for every auth mode of the provider): `openai` → `OPENAI_BASE_URL=https://api.openai.com/v1`, `OPENAI_API_KEY=aurora-placeholder`; `azure_openai` → `OPENAI_BASE_URL=<base>/openai/v1`, `OPENAI_API_KEY=aurora-placeholder`; `openai_compatible` → OpenAI dialects as `openai` with `<base>/v1`, `anthropic_messages` → `ANTHROPIC_BASE_URL=<base>`, `ANTHROPIC_AUTH_TOKEN=aurora-placeholder`; `bedrock` `anthropic_bedrock` → `CLAUDE_CODE_USE_BEDROCK=1`, `AWS_REGION`, `ANTHROPIC_BEDROCK_BASE_URL=<runtime base>`, `CLAUDE_CODE_SKIP_BEDROCK_AUTH=1`; `bedrock` OpenAI dialects → `OPENAI_BASE_URL=<runtime base>/openai/v1`, `OPENAI_API_KEY=aurora-placeholder`; `vertex` `anthropic_vertex` → `CLAUDE_CODE_USE_VERTEX=1`, `CLOUD_ML_REGION=<location>`, `ANTHROPIC_VERTEX_PROJECT_ID`, `ANTHROPIC_VERTEX_BASE_URL=https://<host>/v1`, `CLAUDE_CODE_SKIP_VERTEX_AUTH=1`; `vertex` `openai_chat` → `OPENAI_BASE_URL=https://<host>/v1/projects/<p>/locations/<l>/endpoints/openapi`. Exact variable names are pinned next to each CLI version (live suites). No value in `client_env` is ever a credential (a test asserts no key-shaped value).
- C-810 **Model listing** (`GET /v1/secrets/{id}/models`, `GET /v1/agents/{agent}/models`, unchanged shapes): `openai` `GET /v1/models`; `azure_openai` and `vertex` the static `models` (deployment names / model ids; `models_source: "static"`); `bedrock` `ListFoundationModels` + `ListInferenceProfiles` on `bedrock.<region>.amazonaws.com` signed by the API with the key (or the minted session; a Bedrock `api_key` as bearer), merged as inference-profile ids first, then on-demand model ids, falling back to static `models`; `openai_compatible` `models_path`; all filtered by `allowed_models`. Cached 10 min per secret version as today.
- C-811 **Dashboard** (`14`): LLM key form per auth mode (Bedrock: Assume a role (recommended) with the org external id and a trust-policy snippet, Access keys, Bedrock API key; Azure: Resource key, Microsoft Entra application), verified account shown after save, harness badges per endpoint and the "no enabled harness" notice; Admin › LLM endpoints provider fields (on SET-3's dialog), `allowed_paths` hidden for Bedrock/Vertex, `needs_allowed_private` badge, field errors of C-800. Shared primitives only, both themes, 390 px.
- C-812 **Configuration**: provider keys of C-800 under `config.llm.endpoints` in Helm (no new chart value); `llm.dev_upstream_overrides` (`15`, D-260(e)): `{ "<origin>": "<loopback URL>" }`, accepted only with every target loopback and `gateway.insecure_loopback_injection = true`, refused inside Kubernetes, never rendered by the chart (the `no-deployment-defaults` guard lists it), never an instance setting.
- C-813 **Settings additions** (`llm` section, SET-3): C-772 `entries[]` items gain `warnings?: ["needs_allowed_private"]` for a dashboard endpoint whose host resolves (API resolver, 2 s, cached 60 s) into a refused range not covered by `gateway.allowed_private`; the preview (C-774) warns `{ code: "endpoint_resolves_private", key: "llm.endpoints", entry_id, message }` for the same condition on the entries being written; write refusals of C-800 apply to dashboard entries only (Helm entries are validated at start).
- C-814 **Tests** (`18 §Additional LLM providers`): SEC-64, SEC-65, SEC-97, SEC-98 "Proven by"; mock upstreams in `aurora-testkit` per provider and per mint endpoint; every fixed host reached through C-812's override in gateway ITs and e2e.

### Phase 5 — P4-011B (approval park-and-resume and Web Push, D-133, D-134, D-264..D-267)

Rule: `07 §Park and resume` and `§Web Push`, `06 §Parking and grants` and §Guidance responses, `05` (Approvals, Web Push, Background jobs, `/v1/instance`), `03 §Planned changes`, `10 §Turns` and §Transcript events, `12`, `13 §5.6`, `14`, `15` (§Instance settings, §Configuration reference, §Approval park-and-resume and Web Push), `16 §W16`, `23`, `02 §Limits`, SEC-74, SEC-75, SEC-76, SEC-102. Reserved numbers C-820..C-839. Migrations `20261201000220..223` used, `…224..229` reserved and unused. Not built yet (briefs P4-011B-1..5). Errors use the standard body. Nothing changes for a deployment that keeps `approvals.park.enabled = false` and `push.enabled = false`, except the additive DTO fields and request-log columns (always present, `false`/`null`).

- C-820 **Parked refusal** (gateway, step 8; P4-011B-1). Eligibility exactly `07 §Park and resume` *Which approvals park* 1–5, plus: the request is not to a governed LLM endpoint (D-264(c)). Answer 403 `approval_parked`, header `x-should-retry: false`, `details: { approval_id, expires_at, resume_window_secs, manage_url }` (`manage_url` = `<external>/o/<org>/w/<ws>/approvals?focus=<id>`), `message` = the guidance text of `07` with the window in minutes. An identical request (same fingerprint under the pending row's salt, same agent and conversation) while the row is `pending` → the same answer with the same `approval_id`; nothing is inserted or notified. Caps → 403 `approval_limit_reached` `details: { limit: int, per: "pending" | "hour" }`, `x-should-retry: false`. A repeat of a fingerprint denied within `approvals.park.deny_cooldown_secs` → 403 `manual_approval_denied` `details: { approval_id, cooldown_until }` and no new approval. A store failure → 503 `approval_store_unavailable`. Never a body, header or query value in any `details`.
- C-821 **Fingerprint v1** (`aurora_policy::park::fingerprint`, one implementation, gateway only; checked-in vectors). `canonical = "aurora-park-v1\0" ‖ agent_id (16 bytes) ‖ conversation_id (16) ‖ F(method, ASCII upper-case) ‖ F(host lower-case + ":" + port in decimal) ‖ F(path as normalized by step 3) ‖ F(raw query after "?", byte-exact, "" when absent; order and encoding significant) ‖ SHA-256(complete body; of the empty string when none) (32) ‖ requiring rule `logical_id` (16) ‖ F("connection" | "secret" | "none") ‖ winning credential id (16; zeros for none) ‖ F(tool id or "")`, where `F(x) = u32 big-endian length ‖ UTF-8 bytes`. `fingerprint = SHA-256(fp_salt ‖ canonical)`, `fp_salt` = 16 bytes from the OS CSPRNG per row. Comparison is constant-time. The canonical bytes are never stored or logged.
- C-822 **Approval DTOs** (additive; P4-011B-2). `Approval` (workspace list, `approvals/pending`, history, `approvals/stream` `created`/`decided` events) gains `parked: bool`, `resume_state: "ready" | "consumed" | "expired" | "revoked" | null`, `resume_expires_at: string | null`, `consumed_at: string | null`; the C-263 `notice` gains `parked: bool`. `POST /v1/approvals/{id}/decision` and the batch route: for a parked approval approved, the response's approval carries `resume: { state: "ready", expires_at }`. `fingerprint` and `fp_salt` are never in any DTO, OpenAPI schema, log or audit row. The decider rules (`can_approve`, conversation visibility, groups) and 409 `already_decided`, including FIX-109's deadline-in-the-decision, are unchanged.
- C-823 **Decision effects** (API, one transaction with the decision and its critical audit row; P4-011B-2). Approve: `status = approved`, `resume_state = 'ready'`, `resume_expires_at = decided_at + approvals.park.resume_window_secs`, the follow-up of C-824 inserted and `resume_turn_id` set. Deny: `status = denied` and a "denied" follow-up. In both cases, when the agent is effectively frozen or the conversation is gone, no follow-up is created and an approve sets `resume_state = 'revoked'` instead of `ready`. `approval.decide` audit metadata gains `parked: true` and, for an approve, `resume_expires_at`. Expiry at `deadline` (job, C-828): `expired`, `approval.expired { reason: "deadline" }`, notice, no turn.
- C-824 **Resume follow-up** (P4-011B-2). One per conversation per deciding transaction (batch decisions included), listing at most `PARK_FOLLOWUP_LINES_MAX` (10) approvals then "…and N more". Steered as a `joining` follow-up when the origin turn is still running and its harness declares `steer`; otherwise a `queued` turn with `speaker_kind = system`, no speaker, door `{ "kind": "system", "ref": "approval:<first approval id>" }`. Text (platform voice; each field `one_line` ≤ 120; built only from the stored sanitized summary title, method, host and redacted path, the decider's label and times): approved — `Aurora: a person approved a request you were waiting for.` then per approval `- Approved by <label>: <title> — <METHOD> <host><path>` then `Repeat exactly the same request once, byte for byte, before <HH:MM> UTC (<N> minutes). A changed request needs a new approval.`; denied — `Aurora: a person denied a request you were waiting for.` then `- Denied by <label>: <title> — <METHOD> <host><path>` then `Do not try it again or look for another way to do it; tell the user it was denied.`. A transaction with both kinds writes both blocks in one follow-up. The follow-up text is never posted to a chat platform (`13`: doorless turns post the agent's answer only).
- C-825 **Transcript events, notices and cards** (P4-011B-2). `approval.pending` gains `{ expires_at, parked }`; `approval.decided` for a parked approval gains `{ resume_expires_at }`; `approval.expired` gains `{ reason: "deadline" | "resume_window" }`. Platform `notice` codes: `approval_window_ended` ("The approved request was not repeated in time; a new approval is needed"), `approval_grant_revoked` with `text` naming the cause (`frozen`, `access_removed`, `session_reset`), `approval_abandoned` (a pending parked approval abandoned by C-826). Cards (`13 §5.6`): the parked sentence of `13`, settled on approve ("Approved by X — <agent> will be told"), deny, deadline expiry, abandonment and, by editing the settled card, "approval window ended" or "revoked"; a card in a shared space still carries only title, agent, rule and expiry (D-192); the reminder is never a card.
- C-826 **Revocation hooks** (P4-011B-2, D-264(d)). In the transaction of each event, with the predicate the event already uses for `pending` approvals (FIX-084): freeze of agent/workspace/org; grant deletion or narrowing; connection or secret disabled or deleted; activation of a snapshot that would block — `pending` parked rows → `abandoned` (as held rows), `ready` grants → `resume_state = 'revoked'`. Additionally, for the conversation (or every conversation of the agent): agent deletion, conversation deletion, `harness_session_epoch` bump (harness switch, `reset_session` redaction) → pending parked rows `abandoned`, `ready` grants `revoked`. `resolved_at` is set on every terminal transition as today. Unfreeze revives nothing.
- C-827 **Request log** (P4-011B-1, D-265(a)). Columns `approval_id uuid null`, `resumed bool not null default false`; decision CHECK gains `approval_parked`. Mapping: parked refusal and identical repeat → `approval_parked` (status 403, `error_code` `approval_parked`, `approval_id`); consumed grant → `approved`, `resumed = true`, `approval_id`; hold → `approval_id` set on its row (any outcome); cooldown → `approval_denied`; caps → `rate_limited` with `error_code` `approval_limit_reached`. Activity DTO (`GET /v1/workspaces/{ws}/activity`, request rows) gains `approval_id: string | null` and `resumed: bool`; the decision filter accepts `approval_parked`.
- C-828 **Jobs, reminders, metrics** (P4-011B-2 except the push stage, C-834). Expiry job: `pending AND parked AND deadline ≤ now()` → `expired` (no 30 s grace); stale-holder sweep and SIGTERM never touch `parked` rows. Resume-window job: `resume_state = 'ready' AND resume_expires_at ≤ now()` → `expired` + `approval.expired { reason: "resume_window" }` + `approval_window_ended` notice + card edit, no turn. Reminder: once per (approval, user) when `now ≥ created_at + (deadline − created_at) / 2` and still `pending`: e-mail with claim `approval_notifications (approval_id, user_id, stage = 'reminder')` to deciders with `approval_email` on and no live watcher in that workspace (whatever the initial delivery was; same content rules as C-265 plus "the agent has stopped waiting"). Metrics: `aurora_gateway_approvals_parked_total`, `aurora_gateway_approval_grants_total{result="consumed"|"no_match"|"expired"|"revoked"}`, `aurora_approvals_parked_pending` (gauge, API), `aurora_approvals_resume_expired_total`, `aurora_approvals_grants_revoked_total{cause}`.
- C-829 **Settings and instance facts** (P4-011B-2, D-265(c)(d)). Registry (`approvals` section) entries, all `helm_key` `config.approvals.park.<key>`: `approvals.park.enabled` bool false (loosening `true`); `threshold_secs` duration 60–`approvals.park.max_timeout_secs`, 900 (loosening `down`); `max_timeout_secs` 3600–604800, 86400 (`up`); `resume_window_secs` 60–86400, 1800 (`up`); `deny_cooldown_secs` 0–86400, 900 (`down`); `max_body_bytes` 4096–1048576, 262144 (`up`); `max_pending_per_agent` 1–200, 20 (`up`). Cross-key: `threshold_secs ≤ approvals.max_timeout_secs ≤ max_timeout_secs` → else 422 `settings_invalid` field codes `park_threshold_above_hold_max` / `park_max_below_hold_max` (startup refuses the same, naming the keys). Rule and grant timeouts (C-260/C-261): bound `[60, park.enabled ? park.max_timeout_secs : approvals.max_timeout_secs]`, `details.max` added to `approval_timeout_out_of_range`. `Simulation` and `EffectiveTool` gain `approval_parks: bool` and `approval_timeout_clamped: bool`. New **`GET /v1/policy/approval-limits`** (S/K, any signed-in user; not on the unauthenticated `/v1/instance`) → `{ timeout_secs, max_timeout_secs, park: { enabled, threshold_secs, max_timeout_secs, resume_window_secs } }`, the live effective values (no other key).
- C-830 **`GET /v1/push/config`** (S; P4-011B-3) → `{ available: bool, vapid_public_key: string | null, allowed_hosts: string[] }`; `available` = `push.enabled` and a usable key; `vapid_public_key` = base64url of the 65-byte uncompressed P-256 point of the active key (generated or operator-managed). 404 `push_unavailable` while `push.enabled = false` (every route of C-831 too). `GET /v1/instance` `features.push: bool`.
- C-831 **Subscriptions** (`/v1/me/push-subscriptions`; session only — an API key gets 403 `session_required`; own rows only, another person's id is 404; P4-011B-3). `PushSubscription = { id, label, show_details, paused, state: "active" | "paused" | "disabled" | "expired" | "stale_key", this_session: bool, created_at, last_success_at: string | null, last_failure_at: string | null }` — never the endpoint, host-qualified URL or keys. `GET` → `{ subscriptions: PushSubscription[] }`. `POST` `{ endpoint, keys: { p256dh, auth }, expiration_time?: number | null, label?: string ≤ 80, show_details?: bool, vapid_public_key: string, rebind?: bool }` → 201 `PushSubscription` (200 when the endpoint already belonged to the caller: session re-bound, label/flags kept unless given). `rebind: true` succeeds only when the endpoint is already registered to the caller (else 404 `push_subscription_not_found`, nothing created). Errors: 422 `push_endpoint_not_allowed` (`details.reason`: `not_https`, `host_not_allowed`, `too_long`, `userinfo`, `ip_literal`), 422 `push_keys_invalid` (`p256dh` not a 65-byte point on P-256, `auth` not 16 bytes), 409 `push_subscription_limit_reached` (`PUSH_SUBSCRIPTIONS_PER_USER`), 409 `push_key_stale` (`vapid_public_key` is not the active key: re-fetch config, re-subscribe). An endpoint registered by another person moves to the caller only on a request without `rebind`. `PATCH /{id}` `{ label?, show_details?, paused? }` → `PushSubscription`; `DELETE /{id}` → 204, idempotent; `POST /{id}/test` → 202 (`PUSH_TEST_PER_HOUR`, 429 `push_test_rate_limited` with `Retry-After`), outcome visible in the row. Rate class `write` for the writes.
- C-832 **Payload v1** (P4-011B-3; `16 §W16`). Plaintext JSON ≤ `PUSH_PAYLOAD_MAX`: `{ "v": 1, "type": "approval.pending" | "approval.reminder" | "approval.digest" | "test", "approval_id"?, "workspace_id"?, "expires_at"?, "parked"?, "url"?, "count"?, "agent"?, "workspace"? }`. `approval.pending`/`approval.reminder`: `approval_id`, `workspace_id`, `expires_at`, `parked`, `url` = `/o/<org slug>/w/<ws slug>/approvals?focus=<approval id>`; `approval.digest`: `count`, `url` = `/o/<org slug>/w/<ws slug>/approvals` of the newest one; `test`: nothing else. `agent`/`workspace` (sanitized `one_line` ≤ 40) only when the subscription's `show_details`. Headers: `TTL` = min(remaining approval time, `PUSH_TTL_MAX`), ≥ 60 (test and digest: 3600); `Urgency: high` for pending and reminder, `normal` otherwise; `Topic` = first 32 characters of base64url(SHA-256(subscription id bytes ‖ approval id bytes)), for a digest SHA-256(subscription id ‖ "digest"). Encryption RFC 8291 `aes128gcm`, one record, fresh ephemeral key and salt per message.
- C-833 **VAPID keys** (P4-011B-3). Generated only when `push.enabled` and no operator key: the first API replica that finds no `active` row takes `pg_advisory_xact_lock(hashtext('aurora.webpush.vapid'))`, re-checks, generates a P-256 key with the OS CSPRNG, seals the PKCS#8 private key (purpose `webpush.vapid`, **`inline` store**, owner = key id, org = nil UUID) and inserts the row. JWT: header `{ "typ": "JWT", "alg": "ES256" }`, claims `aud` = the endpoint's scheme+host(+port), `exp` = now + `PUSH_VAPID_JWT_TTL` (1 h), `sub` = `push.vapid.subject`; cached per (key, `aud`) until 5 min before `exp`. Operator key (`push.vapid.private_key_env`): PKCS#8 PEM or base64url raw 32-byte scalar; invalid → startup refusal naming the variable; never stored. CLI `aurora-api push rotate-vapid [--dry-run]` (generated keys only; exit 3 with an operator key): new `active`, old `retired` with `retired_at`; critical audit `push.vapid_rotate` (instance, source `cli`). `aurora-api push status [--json]`: `{ mode: "generated" | "operator" | "disabled", keys: [{ id, status, created_at, retired_at, subscriptions }], subscriptions_by_state, subscriptions_by_host }`, never key material. Daily job destroys a retired key and its sealed secret when unreferenced or older than `PUSH_VAPID_RETIRE` (its subscriptions are deleted).
- C-834 **Delivery** (API job; P4-011B-3). Trigger: `aurora_approval created` (gateway, parked or action approval) + `APPROVAL_PUSH_DELAY`, and a 10 s catch-up for approvals ≤ 10 min old; reminder stage per C-828's timing for parked approvals. Audience at send time: users who are deciders of the approval (`aurora-db::authz`, groups, conversation visibility — the C-265 set computed live), **with no live watcher in its workspace**, owning a subscription that is not paused, not disabled, not expired, whose `vapid_public_key` is servable, and whose `session_id` passes the session liveness check (not revoked, before absolute expiry, inside the idle window). Re-check `status = 'pending'` immediately before claiming. Claim `approval_push_claims (approval_id, user_id, stage)` insert-on-conflict; batch: one push per `batch_id` (claims `delivery = 'batch'` for the others); per-user cap `PUSH_PER_USER_HOUR` → the next becomes one `approval.digest` (claim `digest`), the rest folded silently until the hour ends. Send per subscription through the destination guard (allow-list re-check, public addresses, pinned resolution, no redirects, `push.timeout_secs`, pool `push.concurrency`). Outcomes (metric `aurora_push_sent_total{result}`): 201 `sent`; 404/410 `gone` → row deleted; 400/413 `rejected`; 403 `forbidden`; 429/5xx `throttled`/`error` → up to `PUSH_RETRY_MAX` tries within 60 s honouring `Retry-After` ≤ 60 s; DNS/connect/TLS `error`; time-out `timeout`; host off the allow-list at send `rejected_host` (row disabled). Consecutive failures reach `PUSH_DISABLE_AFTER_FAILURES` → `disabled_at`. Never affects the approval, its decision or the e-mail path.
- C-835 **Service worker and manifest** (web; P4-011B-4; D-267(a)). `web/public/aurora-push-sw.js`, scope `/`, plain JS (no bundler output, no imports). Response headers: `Cache-Control: no-cache`, `Content-Type: text/javascript; charset=utf-8`, the dashboard CSP. Handlers: `install` → `self.skipWaiting()`; `push` → parse defensively (invalid JSON, `v ≠ 1` or unknown `type` → generic text), `event.waitUntil(registration.showNotification(title, { body, tag, data: { url }, icon: "/icon-192.png", requireInteraction: false }))` where `tag` = `approval_id` or `aurora-digest` or `aurora-test`; text: pending/reminder title "Aurora — an approval needs you" (with names: "<agent> in <workspace> needs an approval"), body "Open Aurora to review it." (parked adds " The agent has stopped waiting."; reminder title "Aurora — an approval is still waiting"); digest "Aurora — <count> approvals waiting"; test "Aurora — test notification" / "Notifications work on this device."; `notificationclick` → close, `url` accepted only if it matches `^/o/[A-Za-z0-9._~%/-]+(\?[A-Za-z0-9._~%=&-]*)?$` and contains neither `//`, `\` nor a `.` or `..` path segment, else `/`; focus an existing same-origin client and `navigate(url)`, else `clients.openWindow(url)`; `pushsubscriptionchange` → nothing. No other listeners. `web/public/manifest.webmanifest`: `{ name: "Aurora", short_name: "Aurora", start_url: "/", display: "standalone", icons: [192, 512] }`, linked from the root layout. CSP gains `worker-src 'self'; manifest-src 'self'`.
- C-836 **Dashboard** (P4-011B-4; `14`). On each dashboard load with a local opt-in marker: compare the browser subscription's key with `config.vapid_public_key` (rotated → unsubscribe, re-subscribe, `POST`, delete the old row) and, when the server no longer lists this browser (`this_session` false or absent), `POST … rebind: true` (404 → clear the marker and show the opt-in card). Sign-out deletes this device's row and unsubscribes before the session ends. - C-837 **Configuration and Helm** (P4-011B-3; D-266(a), D-267(b)). Config keys of `15` (`push.enabled`, `push.vapid.subject`, `push.vapid.private_key_env`, `push.allowed_endpoint_hosts`, `push.concurrency`, `push.timeout_secs`) unchanged and Helm-only (`15` "Not settings"); new development key `push.dev_endpoint_overrides` (map host → `http://127.0.0.1:<port>`-style loopback URL): refused unless every target is loopback **and** `server.external_url` is loopback; never rendered by the chart, listed by the `no-deployment-defaults` guard, never in the settings registry. Startup refuses `push.enabled` with an empty or invalid allow-list, an invalid `push.vapid.subject` (`mailto:` or `https:` only), or an unreadable operator key. Chart values: `push.enabled` (bool, default `false`; renders `config.push.enabled` by mirroring and the api egress NetworkPolicy rule), `push.networkPolicy.egressTo` (list of peers, default `[]`; required non-empty when `push.enabled`, render fails otherwise), `push.vapid.existingSecret` / `push.vapid.key` (optional; mounts the key as `AURORA_PUSH_VAPID_KEY` and sets `config.push.vapid.private_key_env`), `push.allowedEndpointHosts` (optional list; rendered into `config.push.allowed_endpoint_hosts` only when set). No chart value for `approvals.park.*` (instance settings; `config.approvals.park.*` locks them).

- C-839 **Tests** (`18 §Approval park-and-resume and Web Push`). `aurora-testkit/src/push.rs`: a loopback mock push service reached through `push.dev_endpoint_overrides` that verifies the VAPID JWT (ES256 signature with the `k=` key, `aud` = the configured real origin, `exp` ≤ now + 1 h, `sub`), decrypts `aes128gcm` with a test subscription key pair (RFC 8291), records `TTL`/`Urgency`/`Topic` and the plaintext, and can answer 201/404/410/400/403/413/429/5xx, delay, or redirect; the RFC 8291 Appendix A and RFC 8292 examples as unit vectors; C-821 fingerprint vectors; the Playwright service-worker leg via `ServiceWorker.deliverPushMessage`; SEC-74/75/76/102 "Proven by" filled by the lanes.
- C-841 **Overview Previews line** (FIX-148, AUDIT-004 F-4). `GET /v1/admin/overview` gains `previews?: { relay_replicas: int, last_seen_at?: Timestamp }` — present only while `previews.enabled`; `relay_replicas` counts the rows `GET /v1/admin/relays` returns (replicas seen in the last 15 minutes), `last_seen_at` is the newest of them (absent with none). The dashboard renders it as the **Previews** line at the top of Admin › Setup, the admin landing page (the dedicated Overview page of `14` is still unbuilt, FIX-048).
- C-842 **Connect dialog: OAuth result over a same-origin channel; connect deep link** (FIX-149, D-272; amends C-14 and I-29).
  - **Callback, success.** The OAuth callback page (same external origin as the dashboard) posts `{ type: "aurora:connected", app_id, connection_id, missing_scopes: string[] }` on `new BroadcastChannel("aurora-connect")`. It is a nonce'd inline script, as before; nothing is sent to `window.opener`.
  - **Callback, failure.** The failure page posts `{ type: "aurora:connect-failed", app_id?, error: <code> }` the same way and still carries `error=<code>` in the page.
  - **Acknowledgement.** The dashboard dialog that is waiting for that `app_id` answers `{ type: "aurora:connect-ack", connection_id?, error? }` on the same channel. On the ack the page calls `window.close()`. Without an ack within 3 s it stays open: it shows "Connected" (or the error) and a link to `/o/<org>/w/<ws>/credentials?tab=connections`.
  - **Message checks.** Messages are not secret (ids and scope names). Receivers check the shape and `type`, and act only on the `app_id` they are waiting for. Every other dashboard tab just refreshes its apps/connections queries.
  - **Opening the sign-in window.** The dashboard opens the window synchronously on the click (`about:blank`). After `POST …/connect` answers `{kind:"redirect", url}`, it sets that window's `opener` to `null` and only then navigates it to `url`. The provider's page never holds a handle on the dashboard (WEBC-3).
  - **Deep link.** The connect deep link is `/o/<org>/w/<ws>/credentials?tab=apps&connect=<app_id>`, which the agent's `request_app_connection` notice (I-29) now emits. `/o/<org>/w/<ws>/apps/<app_id>/connect[?reconnect=<id>]` stays as a redirect to it, so links already sent in conversations still work; `reconnect` maps to `?tab=connections&reconnect=<id>`.
- C-843 **Secret-link lane** (P5-ICS, D-273, SEC-107).
  - **Manifest.** An app may declare `secret_link: { credential: <secret credential name>, expect_prefix?: string (≤ 64) }`. Such an app has no `hosts[]` entry, and its tools are `GET` on `*.link.aurora.internal` path `/`. The linter refuses a non-secret credential, `hosts[]` beside the section, a non-`GET` tool, and the host without the section (`tool_host_uncovered`). `aurora plugin test` accepts `GET https://<uuid>.link.aurora.internal/` with a recorded upstream.
  - **Connect** (`POST …/apps/{app}/connect`, `fields`).
    - The URL must be `https` (`webcal://` normalized), a DNS host without userinfo, and ≤ 2,048 characters.
    - The API fetches it once through the gateway's destination guard and egress policy (10 s, first 256 KiB, redirects as below) and checks `expect_prefix`.
    - It stores the URL as the credential and the URL's host as metadata `host`.
    - Refusals are 422 with `code` ∈ `link_not_https`, `link_invalid`, `link_destination_refused`, `link_not_found`, `link_unreachable`, `link_unexpected_content`; no message contains the URL.
    - No read endpoint ever returns the URL.
  - **Home sync.** `render_inputs.links: [{ name: string, app_id: string, connection_id: uuid }]`: one entry per active connection of a `secret_link` app the agent holds a grant on (the set of `apps[].accounts`). The supervisor writes `~/.aurora/links.json` (`0600`, removed when empty): `[{ name, app, url: "https://<connection_id>.link.aurora.internal/" }]`.
  - **Gateway.**
    - Admission: `<connection_id>.link.aurora.internal` (gateway-CA leaf), `GET`/`HEAD` only (405 otherwise), after policy, grants, approvals and Read only.
    - Forwarding: the request goes to the stored URL exactly. The agent's path and query are ignored, and only `Accept` is forwarded upstream.
    - **Cache** (owner, 2026-11-14).
      - **What is kept:** a successful (`200`), complete upstream body is kept in the serving gateway replica's memory for `SECRET_LINK_CACHE_TTL` (300 s). It is keyed by connection id and credential version, so a reconnect never serves the old link's body.
      - **When it is used:** every request passes the full admission first (grant, policy, approvals, Read only), and only then is it answered from the cache. Each cached answer is still a request-log row, with `cache: hit`.
      - **Bounds:** the total size is bounded by `SECRET_LINK_CACHE_MAX_BYTES` (64 MiB per replica, least recently used evicted). Never on disk; errors and partial bodies are never cached.
      - **Upstream headers are overridden:** `Cache-Control: no-store` from the upstream does not prevent caching, because the cache is private to that connection's admitted callers.
      - **Dropped on:** reconnect, disable or delete of the connection, and on credential revocation.
      - **What the agent gets:** a gateway `ETag` (a hash of the body) and `Age`. An `If-None-Match` matching the cached `ETag` gets `304` without contacting the upstream.
    - Redirects: ≤ 3, `https` only, guard and policy per hop, never returned.
    - Response: streamed and capped at `SECRET_LINK_RESPONSE_MAX` (20 MiB); `Set-Cookie`/`Location` dropped.
    - Errors: 404 `link_not_found`, 403 `link_destination_refused`, 502 `upstream_echoed_credential` (needles: the URL and its path-with-query, raw and percent-encoded).
    - Request log rows and spans carry the connection id and the host, never the path or query.
- C-844 **Calendar invitations** (P5-CALINV, D-274, SEC-108).
  - **Agent tools** (family Calendar, present only with a configured mailer and the org's `calendar_invitations.enabled`, default `true`):
    - `calendar_invite {title, start, end, timezone?, all_day?, location?, description?, repeat?, reminder_minutes?}` → `{ id: uuid, status: "sent" }`;
    - `calendar_invite_update {id, …}` → `{ id, status: "sent" }`;
    - `calendar_invite_cancel {id}` → `{ id, status: "cancelled" }`;
    - `calendar_invite_list {}` → `{ items: [{ id, title, start, end, timezone, status }] }` (≤ 50, newest first; only this agent's invitations to the current person).
  - **Bounds:**
    - `title` ≤ 200, `location` ≤ 200, `description` ≤ 4,000 (plain text);
    - `start`/`end` RFC 3339 local times with an IANA `timezone` (default: the person's profile zone, else the agent's `TZ`), or dates with `all_day`;
    - `end > start`, duration ≤ 14 days, within 5 years;
    - `repeat {freq: daily|weekly|monthly|yearly, interval?: 1–12, by_day?: [MO…SU], count?: 1–200, until?: date}` with exactly one of `count`/`until`;
    - `reminder_minutes` 0–10,080.
  - **Errors:** `invalid_argument` (the field named), `no_person` (no person behind the turn), `no_verified_email`, `not_found`, `rate_limited` (`retry_after_secs`; 20 per person per agent per hour, 60 per person per day), `mail_unavailable`.
  - **Mail:**
    - Sent to the turn's person at their verified email only, from `email.from`.
    - `multipart/mixed`: `text/plain` with a fixed preface, `text/calendar; method=REQUEST|CANCEL; charset=utf-8`, and `invite.ics`.
    - `UID` = `<id>@<instance host>`; `ORGANIZER` = `email.from` with CN `"<agent> (Aurora)"`; `ATTENDEE;RSVP=FALSE`.
  - **Storage:** table `calendar_invitations` (migration `20261201000230`): `id, org_id, workspace_id, agent_id, person_id, conversation_id, uid, sequence, status, title, starts_at, ends_at, timezone, repeat, created_at, updated_at`. No description or location is stored.
  - **Audit:** `calendar.invitation.{sent,updated,cancelled}`, with ids only.
- C-845 **Person-pinned servers (`own_server`)** (P5-CALDAV, D-275, SEC-109).
  - **Manifest:** `own_server: { url_field, hosts_field, inject: [basic|bearer|header stages] }`. An app with it has no `hosts[]`, `instances`, `tenant` or `rewrite_host`. Its tools use host `"{connection}"` and may use WebDAV methods. The linter refuses `{connection}` without the section. `check-builtin.sh` exempts its read tools from the GET-only rule.
  - **Connect** (`fields`):
    - The URL must be `https` with a DNS host (an IP literal only under `allowed_private`).
    - Discovery runs through the destination guard and egress policy: `/.well-known/caldav` (≤ 3 redirects) → `current-user-principal` → `calendar-home-set`.
    - **Stored:** metadata `url` (the home) and `hosts` (exact `host:port` origins used, ≤ 4).
    - **Refusals (422):** `caldav_not_https`, `caldav_destination_refused`, `caldav_unreachable`, `caldav_unauthorized`, `caldav_not_caldav`, `server_host_claimed` (an origin held by a static claim, a registered instance, an LLM endpoint or an Aurora address; checked before any credential is sent), `caldav_needs_oauth` (Google), `caldav_unsupported` (Exchange/Outlook). None echoes a credential.
  - **Gateway:** the app's `inject` stages run only for a request whose origin is exactly one of the connection's `hosts`, over TLS to that port, and never on a host that is (now) claimed, registered or Aurora's. Elsewhere no credential is injected. The `ical` decoder (summaries for `PUT` bodies: SUMMARY, DTSTART/DTEND/TZID, RRULE present, LOCATION, every ATTENDEE) joins `09 §5`.
  - **Home sync:** `render_inputs.servers: [{ name, app_id, connection_id, url }]` for the agent's granted `own_server` connections. The supervisor writes `~/.aurora/servers.json` (`0600`, removed when empty) as `[{ name, app, url }]`.

- C-750 **Terminal harness wrappers** (FIX-138 item 1b, D-247; `11 §The boot document`, `12 §Terminal`). (1) The boot document gains `enabled_harnesses: string[]` (`hosted.enabled_harnesses`; optional on the supervisor side, absent = only `harness.id`). (2) The supervisor writes `/run/aurora/harness.env` (0644, shell-sourceable, **no secret**) at boot and again at every session start: `HARNESSES_ENABLED`, `HTTPS_PROXY`/`HTTP_PROXY` (+ lowercase) = `http://127.0.0.1:3128`, `NO_PROXY`, `SSL_CERT_FILE`/`NODE_EXTRA_CA_CERTS`/`CODEX_CA_CERTIFICATE` = `/run/aurora/ca.pem`, the CLIs' telemetry/auto-update switches, the placeholder `aurora-placeholder` for `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY`/`ANTHROPIC_AUTH_TOKEN`/`OPENAI_API_KEY`, and with a known model `ANTHROPIC_MODEL` (+ `ANTHROPIC_BASE_URL` for a non-Anthropic endpoint) or `AURORA_CODEX_MODEL`/`AURORA_CODEX_BASE_URL`. (3) `/opt/aurora/bin/{claude,codex}` refuse with "`<harness>`: not enabled on this instance" (exit 127) when the binary is absent or the harness is not in `HARNESSES_ENABLED`; otherwise they source the file, set `CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR=/workspace/.aurora/terminal/claude` / `CODEX_HOME=/workspace/.aurora/terminal/codex` and exec the pinned binary. No wire, API route or database change. (4) **Amendment, owner 2026-11-14 (D-247 (e)): the terminal open carries the models.** `PreviewStreamOpenResponse` (runner plane, terminal grants only) gains optional `models: TerminalModels`, and `PtyOpen` (`pty.open`) gains the same optional field (absent = an older API/runner: today's behaviour). `TerminalModels = { claude_code?: TerminalModel, codex?: TerminalModel }`, one entry per terminal harness enabled on the instance; `TerminalModel = { choice?: ModelChoice, unavailable?: "no_key" | "no_model" }` (exactly one present). The API fills it at redemption with the same `model::select` a turn of that harness would run (`fake` excluded); a selection error other than no-key/no-model leaves that harness out and is logged, never failing the open. The runner copies it into `pty.open` unchanged. On `pty.open` the supervisor rewrites `harness.env` **before** spawning the shell; the file has one section per harness: claude-code → `ANTHROPIC_MODEL`, `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY` or (non-Anthropic endpoint) `ANTHROPIC_BASE_URL` + `ANTHROPIC_AUTH_TOKEN`, plus that choice's `client_env`, exactly as `claude_code::config::build_env` sets for a turn; codex → `AURORA_CODEX_MODEL`/`AURORA_CODEX_BASE_URL`; an unavailable harness → `AURORA_CLAUDE_UNAVAILABLE` / `AURORA_CODEX_UNAVAILABLE` with a fixed sentence ("this agent has no usable LLM key for <Harness>" / "no model is chosen for this agent; choose one on its Instructions page"). A turn's session start replaces only its own harness's section. The wrappers print the `…_UNAVAILABLE` sentence and exit 1 when it is set; each wrapper unsets the other harness's variables. Values remain placeholders only (no secret).

### Phase 5 — P5-CERN-ADDONS (the gateway LDAP lane, D-251)

Rule: `06 §LDAP lane`, `09 §3 ldap`, `90 D-251`, SEC-110. Reserved numbers C-760..C-769 (used C-760..C-763). No `/v1` route or schema change (the widening's `kind` is a string: a new value `directory`); no migration.

- C-760 **Manifest `ldap`** (`09 §3 ldap`): `apps[].ldap = { server, port = 636, base_dn, attributes[1..64], max_entries = 50 (1..500) }`, copied into the snapshot's `apps[].ldap` (absent in older snapshots). Linter codes: `ldap_server_invalid`, `ldap_port_invalid`, `ldap_base_dn_invalid`, `ldap_attributes_invalid`, `ldap_max_entries_invalid`, `ldap_database_exclusive`, `ldap_credentials_declared`, `ldap_host_missing`, `ldap_host_foreign`, `ldap_host_inject`. The synthetic host `<app id>.ldap.aurora.internal` is the only `*.aurora.internal` host a package may claim (reserved-host rule exempt), and only for the app that declares `ldap`.
- C-761 **Lane request** `POST https://<app id>.ldap.aurora.internal/search`, `application/json` ≤ 16 KiB: `{ filter: string (RFC 4515, ≤ 1 KiB), attributes?: string[], scope?: "sub"|"one"|"base", base?: string, limit?: integer }`; unknown keys, other paths and other methods are refused. Answer `200 { entries: [{ dn, attributes: { <name>: string[] } }], truncated: boolean }`. Errors (the usual `ApiError` envelope): `400 ldap_request_invalid` (body, filter, attribute, scope, base), `413 ldap_request_invalid` (body > 16 KiB), `403 ldap_no_connection` (no granted connection of the app, or plain HTTP), `404 ldap_operation_unknown`, `502 ldap_unavailable` (dial/TLS/protocol), `502 ldap_refused` (the directory refused the bind or search), `504 ldap_timeout`; a refused destination is `destination_refused`.
- C-762 **Permission diff**: `Widening::Directory { app, server: "host:port", base_dn }` for a new or wider `ldap` block (another server, port or base, an attribute not in the installed block, a larger `max_entries`); `GET /v1/admin/plugins/{id}` `widenings[]` carries `{ kind: "directory", detail: "<app> can read directory entries from <server:port> under <base_dn>" }`. A narrower or identical block is not a widening.
- C-763 **Tests** (`18`): registry (`tests/it/ldap.rs`: shape rules, host exception, snapshot copy, widening); gateway unit (BER codec, filter grammar and refusals, base confinement, query building) and pipeline ITs against a fake LDAPS directory (`tests/it/ldap_lane.rs`); package fixtures of `cern-phonebook` (in `github.com/alexiri/aurora-addons-cern`, `plugins/cern-phonebook/tests`, D-252; the staging copy in this repository is deleted); the kind e2e `k7_cern_addons_source_to_phonebook_search_and_route`.

### Phase 5 — P5-ASRC (addon sources: third-party app-package repositories, D-250)

Rule: `09 §10` (source `addon`), `05 §Instance administration`, `03 §addon_sources`, `14 §Admin`, `15`, `17` SEC-86, `22-addon-repos.md` (public format). Reserved numbers C-590..C-609. Errors use the standard body. All routes are **IA** (instance admin, session), audited, and 403 for anyone else.

- C-590 **`AddonSource`** = `{ id, name, url (the resolved manifest URL), homepage, contact, publisher, key_fingerprint (sha256:12 hex of the manifest-published key), update_interval ("6h"|"12h"|"1d"|"1w"|"2w"|"1mo"|"never"), status ("ok"|"check_failed"|"signing_key_changed"), check_failed_at: timestamp|null, check_failed_reason: string|null (sanitized), new_key_fingerprint: string|null (only in `signing_key_changed`), last_checked_at: timestamp|null, manifest_digest, addons: [AddonEntry], addon_count, key_description: string|null, removed_upstream: [{ package_id, version }], created_at, updated_at }`. **The view is built from the source's stored last-accepted manifest** (`addon_sources.manifest`), never a live fetch: GETs are cheap and the last known state keeps serving while the repository is unreachable; `removed_upstream` = versions this instance holds from the source that the stored manifest no longer lists; `AddonEntry.versions` are newest first. `AddonEntry` = `{ id, name, description, versions: [{ version, min_core: string|null, ingestable: bool, not_ingestable_reason: string|null, installed_version: string|null, update_available: bool }] }` — `installed_version` = the active (else newest ingested) version on the instance; `update_available` = a manifest version is semver-newer than the newest ingested (any status but `invalid`) one.
- C-591 **`GET /v1/admin/addon-sources`** (operation `adminListAddonSources`) → `{ items: [AddonSource] }` (sources are few: no paging), ordered `created_at, id`. `GET /v1/admin/addon-sources/{id}` (operation `adminGetAddonSource`) → the same `AddonSource` (full detail).
