- C-592 **Preview (register)**: `POST /v1/admin/addon-sources/preview` `{ url }` → 200 `AddonSourcePreview` = `{ url (the normalized manifest URL), repo: { name, description, homepage, contact }, publisher, key_fingerprint, key_description, addons: [AddonEntry] (with install state), packages_new: int (what registering would ingest now: C-607) }`; 409 `addon_source_exists` when the URL is registered. `url` is the repo URL or the direct manifest URL: normalized to the manifest URL (a repo URL gains `/aurora-addons.yaml`; a URL already ending in it is kept; a **GitHub or GitLab repository page** becomes the raw URL — `github.com/o/r[/tree/<ref>[/dir]]` → `raw.githubusercontent.com/o/r/<ref|HEAD>/[dir/]aurora-addons.yaml`, `gitlab…/g/r` → `gitlab…/g/r/-/raw/HEAD/aurora-addons.yaml`) and the **normalized URL is stored**, never the pasted one. Fetch errors map: unreachable/timeout 422 `addon_unreachable` (sanitized reason), non-https or private/IP host 422 `addon_url_refused`, manifest > 1 MiB 413, parse/schema/`api` version 422 `addon_manifest_invalid` (details carry one sanitized message), key not a valid ed25519 key 422 `addon_manifest_invalid`.
- C-593 **`POST /v1/admin/addon-sources`** `{ url, update_interval? }` (default: the config key `plugins.addon_sources.update_interval`, itself `1d` unless set) → 201 `AddonSource`. Fetch + validate exactly as C-592 (a failed fetch creates nothing; the manifest's publisher `aurora` is reserved and refused), then **one audited transaction** (503 `audit_unavailable` and nothing written when the critical row fails): (1) the **`trusted_keys`** row `(publisher = manifest.publisher, name = "<repo name> signing key", public_key = manifest key in its canonical spelling, added_by = admin)` — a `trusted_keys_public_key_key` conflict (the same key already trusted) is 409 `addon_key_exists` and aborts registration; (2) the `addon_sources` row (name = manifest `repo.name`, the parsed manifest, the first scheduled check one interval out); (3) critical `addon_source.add`. **Then**, outside that transaction (each ingest is the ordinary pipeline's own transaction and a failing version must not roll the registration back), the listed packages are ingested (C-607); each ingested version writes a critical `addon.ingest` in its own transaction. A version that fails digest, signature, identity or lint is stored as an **`invalid`** package row (`status_reason` = the short code, the sentence in `violations`) and never blocks the others; `min_core` above the running core is listed but not ingested (`ingestable = false`, reason `core_too_new`).
- C-594 **`PATCH /v1/admin/addon-sources/{id}`** `{ update_interval? }` (operation `adminUpdateAddonSource`) → 200 `AddonSource`; an interval outside the enum → 400 `invalid_request`. Critical `addon_source.update` (metadata: `interval: "<old> → <new>"`). `"never"` stops only the schedule; "Check now" stays available.
- C-595 **Key change and re-confirm**: a check that reads a manifest whose `signing_key` differs from the registered fingerprint (1) ingests **nothing**, (2) sets `status = "signing_key_changed"`, `new_key_fingerprint`, (3) audits critical `addon_source.check` outcome `failure` reason `signing_key_changed`. `POST /v1/admin/addon-sources/{id}/confirm-key` `{ new_key_fingerprint }` (operation `adminConfirmAddonSourceKey`; the body echoes the fingerprint the admin saw) → 200 `AddonSource`: re-fetches the manifest and verifies the fingerprint still matches (otherwise 409 `addon_key_changed_again`), updates the source's `key_fingerprint` and **updates the existing `trusted_keys` row's `public_key`** (nothing is revoked; packages signed by the old key stay as they are), audits critical `addon_source.update` metadata `{ change: "signing_key_confirmed", old, new }`. New versions ingest only after the confirm.
- C-596 **Check (the shared poll path)**: `POST /v1/admin/addon-sources/{id}/check` (operation `adminCheckAddonSource`) → 200 `AddonSourceCheckResult` = `{ source_id, status: "updated"|"up_to_date"|"failed", ingested: [PluginPackage], failed_versions: [{ package_id, version, reason }], check_failed_reason: string|null }`; `POST /v1/admin/addon-sources/check-all` (operation `adminCheckAllAddonSources`) → 200 `{ items: [AddonSourceCheckResult] }` — one result per source, checked sequentially, a failing source never blocks the others (its own fetch timeout bounds it). A fetch or parse failure is **a result, not an error**: 200 with `status: "failed"` and the sanitized `check_failed_reason` (the source keeps serving its last state; the failure is also recorded on the source and as a critical `addon_source.check` row with outcome `failure`). Both run **exactly** the scheduled poll path with `trigger = "manual"` in the `addon_source.check` audit row (the job writes `trigger = "scheduled"`), under the source's lease: a concurrent check of the same source is refused 409 `addon_check_in_progress`; check-all skips a busy source with `status: "failed", check_failed_reason: "busy"`. Audit: one critical `addon_source.check` per source plus the per-version `addon.ingest` rows.
- C-597 **`DELETE /v1/admin/addon-sources/{id}`** (operation `adminDeleteAddonSource`) → 204; **409 `addon_source_active`** while any package of the source is `active` (`pending_approval` rows do not block: reject them instead). On success the source row is deleted and **its packages stay** (still governed by their trusted key), polling stops (the UI confirm states both). Critical `addon_source.remove` (metadata: url, package count that stays).
- C-598 **The poll job** (`05 §Background jobs`, like the library refresh): a ticker claims due sources — `next_check_at <= now()`, lease free — with `FOR UPDATE SKIP LOCKED`, lease `ADDON_LEASE` 5 min, at most `ADDON_CHECK_CONCURRENCY` 2 per replica; interval from `plugins.addon_sources.update_interval` (`never` → sources are never due); ±10 % jitter on the next `next_check_at`; a failure backs off 1 h. Per source the shared path of C-596 with `trigger = "scheduled"`; a `signing_key_changed` source is claimed only to re-read the manifest (the state stays until C-595).
- C-599 **Errors**: 409 `addon_key_exists` (C-593), `addon_source_exists` (C-592/C-593: the URL is registered), `addon_source_active` (C-597), `addon_check_in_progress` (C-596), `addon_key_changed_again` (C-595), `addon_key_not_changed` (confirm on a source whose key did not change), `addon_key_revoked` (confirm when the source's trusted key was revoked: register again); 422 `addon_url_refused`, `addon_unreachable`, `addon_manifest_invalid` (C-592; details one sanitized reason); 413 manifest > 1 MiB. Fetch reasons are sanitized (no URL query strings, no body text) before storage in `check_failed_reason` (≤ 200 chars).
- C-600 **Plugins list provenance and badges**: `PluginPackage` gains `source_id: uuid|null` (set for `source = "addon"`), `source_name: string|null` (the addon source's name at read time; never stored), `update_available: bool` (true iff a version the source's stored manifest lists — ingestable on this core — is semver-newer than this row, and the row is the one in use (`active`) or the newest version the instance holds (`pending_approval`); so while a newer version waits for approval the active row carries the badge; always false for non-addon rows). Both are set on `GET /v1/admin/plugins`; OpenAPI and `schema.d.ts` regenerated.
- C-601 **Admin UI — Addon sources** (`/admin/addon-sources`, `14 §Admin`): list with name, URL, publisher, key fingerprint (monospace, copyable), schedule, last checked + status chip (`ok` / `check_failed` with reason / `signing_key_changed` banner), addon count. **Add** opens the C-592 preview as a review screen (repo info, publisher, the signing-key fingerprint prominently, the addon list with versions, "N packages are new to this instance") and submits C-593. Row actions: **Check now** (C-596, disabled while running), **Edit schedule** (C-594; options exactly the enum), **Remove** (C-597; the confirm states packages stay and polling stops; blocked while packages are active), **Re-confirm key** (only in `signing_key_changed`: old + new fingerprint side by side, C-595). The Add dialog links the format documentation (`22-addon-repos.md`).
- C-602 **Admin UI — Plugins badges** (`14 §Admin`): the plugins list gains a **Source** column (`builtin`/`directory`/`upload`/the addon source's name), **Update available** badges on addon rows with `update_available`, and a **"Check for updates"** button (C-596 check-all) visible only when at least one addon source is registered; per-source outcomes surface as toasts (updated / up to date / failed + reason). A badge's detail points to the addon's entry in the source detail view.
- C-603 **Config**: `[plugins.addon_sources] update_interval` (default `"1d"`, enum as C-590; `15`): the schedule a **newly registered** source starts with (C-593; an admin changes a source's own schedule with C-594); validated at startup (an invalid value refuses startup naming the key). Limits are compile-time (`02 §Limits`).
- C-604 **Data**: migration `20261201000180_addon_sources.sql`: `addon_sources` (C-605), `plugin_packages.source_id uuid null FK→addon_sources (set null)`, the `source` CHECK gains `'addon'`, index `(source_id) WHERE source_id IS NOT NULL`. `03` gains the real `addon_sources` section and `plugin_packages.source_id`. The gateway role gets **nothing** on `addon_sources` (it reads packages through `gateway_plugin_packages` as before).
- C-605 **`addon_sources`** = `{ id uuid pk, name text not null, url text not null unique (the resolved manifest URL), homepage text null, contact text null, publisher text not null, signing_key text not null (base64 ed25519), key_fingerprint text not null, new_key_fingerprint text null, manifest_digest text not null, manifest jsonb not null (the last accepted manifest as parsed), update_interval text not null CHECK in (`6h`,`12h`,`1d`,`1w`,`2w`,`1mo`,`never`) default `1d`, status text not null CHECK in (`ok`,`check_failed`,`signing_key_changed`) default `ok`, check_failed_at timestamptz null, check_failed_reason text null, last_checked_at timestamptz null, next_check_at timestamptz not null default now(), check_lease_until timestamptz null, lease_owner uuid null, created_by uuid null FK→users (set null), created_at/updated_at (implicit per `03 §Conventions`) }`.
- C-606 **Manifest format** (`22-addon-repos.md` is normative): YAML, `api: 1` (integer; any other value 422 `addon_manifest_invalid` — a newer `api` means "upgrade Aurora"), strict parse (unknown fields refused): `repo: { name (1..80), description (≤ 280), homepage (https URL, optional), contact (https or mailto URL, optional) }`, `publisher` (valid package publisher id), `signing_key` (base64 ed25519), `signing_key_description` (≤ 80, optional), `addons[]` (≤ 100): `{ id (valid package id), name (1..80), description (≤ 280), versions: map of semver → { package (https URL), sha256 (64 hex), size_bytes (≤ `PACKAGE_MAX`, advisory), min_core (semver, optional) } }` (≤ 50 versions per addon).
- C-607 **Package selection**: for every addon entry the versions semver-newer than the newest ingested (any status but `invalid`, any source: package ids are unique on the instance) version of that `package_id` — or the newest ingestable listed one when none was ever ingested — are candidates, ascending; a candidate with `min_core` above the running core is skipped (`ingestable = false`, reason `core_too_new`); a version this source already stored `invalid` is not retried. Each remaining candidate is downloaded (package URL, ≤ 1 redirect, https, public host, ≤ `PACKAGE_MAX`), **digest-checked against the manifest's `sha256` before anything is parsed**, then ingested through the ordinary pipeline with `source = "addon"`, `source_id = <row>` and three addon rules on top (SEC-86): the signature must verify against **the source's own key** (the `trusted_keys` row it registered — not the project key, not another key trusted for the same publisher, not unsigned), the package's `publisher` must be the manifest's, and its `id`/`version` must be the ones the manifest lists; and its package id must not belong to another source, another publisher or a non-addon package. Reasons: `digest_mismatch`, `signer_not_source_key`, `publisher_mismatch`, `identity_mismatch`, `package_id_taken`, or the linter's violation code. One addon-specific override: a first addon version is **always** `pending_approval` (reason `first_install`) — third-party permissions are never auto-activated (D-250(b)); the ordinary holds (`claim_conflict`, `breaks_connections`, `converts_grants`, widened permissions) apply unchanged.
- C-608 **Audit**: `addon_source.add`, `addon_source.update` (interval change, key re-confirm), `addon_source.remove`, `addon_source.check` (per source per run, both triggers), `addon.ingest` (per ingested version; the `plugin.ingest` shape with `source: "addon"`, `source_id`) join the security-critical set (`04 §Audit`): written with `commit_critical` in the change's transaction (503 `audit_unavailable` and no change on failure); a failed check writes its `check` row with outcome `failure`.
- C-609 **Tests** (`18`): API — ingestion happy path (source + trusted key + packages in one transaction, audited), bad signature (that version `invalid`, others fine), digest mismatch, oversized package, key change (state, refusal, confirm), poll state machine (unchanged / new versions / removed upstream / unreachable / `never`), trusted-keys composition (the source's key verifies its packages; revoking it disables them), `min_core` gating, removal rules (409 while active, packages stay), check-all with leases, permission-matrix rows (all routes IA-only), audit coverage (the five actions in the source scan). Web — vitest for the source page and badges; Playwright stage-4 routes with mocks for the C-590..C-602 surface.

### Phase 5 — P4-NARR / P4-UTOK (live narration cards; user tokens and `send_as_me`, D-279..D-284)

Rule: `10 §Live narration cards`, `13 §4.2, §4.5, §5.2, §5.10, §5.11, §5.12`, `20 §Operations, §Rendering`, `05` (rows of this section), `03 §Planned changes` (`agents.narration_cards`, `user_tokens`, `user_token_agents`), `08 §1`, `02 §Limits`, SEC-111..SEC-113. Reserved numbers C-846..C-859. Errors use the standard body. Narration needs no flag: the setting is accepted everywhere and a card appears only where the connector declares `edits`. The user-token routes answer 422 `user_tokens_unsupported` when the integration's connector does not declare `user_tokens`.

- C-846 **Agent setting** (P4-NARR): `PATCH /v1/agents/{agent}` accepts `narration_cards: boolean` (default `false`; `GET` returns it). Non-boolean → 422 `invalid`. Other settings' rules apply (`can_own`). Migration `20261201000231`.
- C-847 **Narration card content** (P4-NARR): a neutral content kind `narration`, a platform-authored card rather than an agent message body: `{ kind: "narration", turn_id, status: "running" | "done" | "failed", steps: [{ name: string ≤ NARR_STEP_CHARS, detail?: string ≤ NARR_STEP_CHARS, status: "running" | "ok" | "failed" }] (the last NARR_MAX_STEPS), earlier: integer ≥ 0 }`; (D-286) the **collapsed** form, sent once at completion, is `{ kind: "narration", turn_id, status: "done" | "failed" | "stopped", collapsed: true, step_count: integer ≥ 0, failed_count: integer ≥ 0, elapsed_ms: integer ≥ 0 }` with no `steps`. `name` is the tool's name from `tool.started`; `detail` is that event's sanitized `summary` and is present only in a `direct` conversation with the agent (C-850). A connector renders it as data (`13 §4.3`), never as agent Markdown.
- C-848 **Card lifecycle** (P4-NARR): only the existing ops, no new kind. Creation `message.post` with `op_marker` `narr:<turn_id>` (in place of the receipt, `13 §5.12`); changes `message.update`; collapse: a last `message.update` with the collapsed content and `dedupe_key narr:<turn_id>:end` when the answer posts or the turn ends (D-286; never superseded or dropped, retried on `rate_limited`; no `message.delete`). Each write is keyed by `turn_id` and event sequence (`narr:<turn_id>:<seq>`), so a restarted projector never posts a second card.
- C-849 **Cadence and budget** (P4-NARR): at most one update per turn per `NARR_MIN_INTERVAL`; at most `NARR_SPACE_UPDATES_PER_MINUTE` narration writes per space per minute; at most `NARR_CARDS_PER_SPACE` cards per space at a time (a further turn gets none). A superseded pending update is dropped. `rate_limited` on a card write sets `not_before` as `13 §4.2` and drops that update; the next state replaces it.
- C-850 **Events and privacy** (P4-NARR): the projector reads only `turn.started`, `turn.finished`, `tool.started` and `tool.finished` (`10 §Transcript events`). `activity`, `thinking.delta` and `text.delta` never reach a card. In a conversation that is not `direct`, `detail` is absent. No raw arguments, attachment names or answer text appear in any card field (SEC-111).
- C-851 **Mattermost rendering** (P4-NARR, `20 §Rendering`): a card is a post with the header `Working on it`, `Done` or `Stopped`, then one `- <name>: running|done|failed` line per step (name in a code span, `detail` in a `direct` conversation only), then `- N earlier` when cut. Words only, no emoji, no ping, no link: a bare URL in a label is wrapped in a code span (D-270). A user-token post carries the footer `_Sent by <agent name> for <owner label>_` (C-857).
- C-852 **Dashboard switch** (P4-NARR, `14`): the agent's **Channels** page has **Show progress in chat**, bound to `narration_cards` through C-846; off by default; help text as in `14`. The dashboard chat has no card.
- C-853 **User-token routes** (P4-UTOK): `GET /v1/me/user-tokens` (S/K) → `{ tokens: UserToken[] }`, the caller's own only. `POST /v1/me/user-tokens` (SO) `{ integration_id, token: string ≤ 256, label?: string ≤ 80 }` → 201 `UserToken`: the token is sealed (purpose `user_token`) and verified by `user_token.verify` (C-854) before the response; the value is never returned or logged (the request log omits this body). Refusals: 422 `user_token_mismatch` (the verified platform user is not the caller's own link on the integration), 422 `user_token_invalid` (the platform rejected it), 422 `user_tokens_unsupported`, 409 `user_token_limit` (`USER_TOKENS_PER_PERSON`), 503 `secret_store_unavailable`; in every refusal nothing is stored and the sealed value is discarded. Audit `user_token.create` on success.
- C-854 **Verify op** (P4-UTOK, `13 §4.2`): `user_token.verify` is a correlated op (the 10 s wait of `13 §4.2`) with payload `{ config, user_credential_ref }` (a copy with purpose `channel_op_user_token`, owner = op id) → `{ platform_user_ref, handle }`. A `401` answers `credential_dead`, which the API maps to 422 `user_token_invalid`.
- C-855 **UserToken and grants** (P4-UTOK): `UserToken = { id, integration: { id, type }, label: string | null, platform_user_label: string, status: "active" | "invalid" | "revoked", agents: [{ id, name }], created_at, last_used_at: string | null }`. `PATCH /v1/me/user-tokens/{id}` (SO) `{ label }` → 200. `DELETE /v1/me/user-tokens/{id}` (SO) → 204: revokes (D-284(a)): discards `token_ref`, cancels pending approvals (rejected, `credential_revoked`), refuses copies at their next claim; audit `user_token.revoke` (critical). `PUT /v1/me/user-tokens/{id}/agents/{agent}` (SO) → 204, idempotent; needs `can_use` on the agent (404 for an agent the caller cannot see, 403 for a visible one the caller may not use); audit `user_token.grant`. `DELETE` of the same path → 204, audit `user_token.ungrant`. Another person's token → 404 everywhere.
- C-856 **`send_as_me` tool and approval** (P4-UTOK, `13 §5.11`, `07 §Action approvals`): agent tool `send_as_me { to, text }`. Refusal codes to the agent: `not_eligible`, `not_granted`, `token_inactive` (D-283(a)). A permitted call creates an action approval (`action_key: "send_as_me"`, frozen payload `{ token_id, conversation_id, text }`, platform-composed summary "Post as <owner label> in <conversation label> (<n> characters)", never the text), decided only by the token's owner (any other decider → 403 `not_token_owner`). It expires at `APPROVAL_TIMEOUT`. At most `USER_TOKEN_PENDING_MAX` pending per token (beyond it, refused `pending_limit`). No `allow` mode exists.
- C-857 **Send op** (P4-UTOK, `13 §4.2`): `message.post` with `sender: { user_token: <user_tokens id> }`. Its payload carries `user_credential_ref` (purpose `channel_op_user_token`, owner = op id), opened at claim and delivered as `user_credential`, discarded at the terminal state. `ephemeral_to` and `files` are refused with `invalid`. The connector appends the footer of C-851. A `credential_dead` answer sets the token `invalid` (not the presence), and the op fails with the token's status.
- C-858 **Audit, revocation, limits** (P4-UTOK, D-284): audit actions `user_token.create`, `user_token.revoke` (critical), `user_token.invalid` (system), `user_token.grant`, `user_token.ungrant`, `user_token.send` (one per attempted send: outcome, agent id, token id, conversation id; never text, token or platform user id by value). Revocation triggers: owner `DELETE`, grant or link removal, SCIM deprovision or deletion of the owner, deletion of the agent or the integration, platform `401` (→ `invalid`). Limits `USER_TOKENS_PER_PERSON` and `USER_TOKEN_PENDING_MAX` (`02`).
- C-859 **Tests and dashboard card** (`18 §Live narration cards`, `18 §User tokens`, `14` Account, Send as me): per SEC-111..SEC-113 "Proven by"; the dashboard Account section lists tokens with the shared `ResourceTable`, `Dialog` and `ConfirmDialog` and the states of `14`; the live Mattermost scenario (P4-UTOK) runs against the pinned dev server of `20`.

### Phase 5 — P4-EVAL (evals: questions, runs as real turns, judge, schedules, D-289..D-294)

Rule: `10 §Evals`, `05 §Evals` (routes and jobs), `03 §Planned changes` (`eval_questions`, `eval_runs`, `eval_results`, `eval_schedules`, `agents` eval columns, `conversations.kind`), `04` matrix (eval rows) and §Audit, `08 §Platform-internal LLM calls` (`PlatformLlm`), `02 §Limits` (`EVAL_*`), `14` (Behaviour › Evals), `18 §Evals`, SEC-114..SEC-116. Reserved numbers C-860..C-874. Errors use the standard body. Hosted agents only (BYO: 422 `eval_not_supported`). No response of a connector, notice or audit row carries an expected answer or an answer. Owner questions Q-EVAL-1..10: every recommendation of `docs/tasks/P4-EVAL-1.md` accepted (owner, 2026-11-15, D-300); the contracts follow them.

- C-860 **Eval conversations** (P4-EVAL, D-290(b)): kind `eval`, `external_ref` `eval:<run_id>:<position>` (one per question of one run), visible to `can_use` like other non-direct kinds, excluded from the default chat list (`?kind=` shows it), never mirrored to a channel, never a thread-link target, never the origin of a report. Deleted with its run (`10 §Archive, delete`).
- C-861 **Questions** (P4-EVAL, D-289(a)): `EvalQuestion = { id, agent: { id, name }, position: int, question: string ≤ EVAL_QUESTION_CHARS, expected_kind: "numeric"|"text"|"judged", expected_number: number|null, tolerance: number|null (≥ 0, numeric only), expected_text: string|null (≤ EVAL_EXPECTED_TEXT_CHARS, text only), criteria: string|null (≤ EVAL_CRITERIA_CHARS, judged only), enabled: bool, version: int, created_at, updated_at }`. `GET /v1/agents/{agent}/eval-questions` → `{ questions: EvalQuestion[] }` in position order. `POST` (`can_use`) → 201; `GET|PATCH|DELETE /v1/eval-questions/{id}` (`can_use`; PATCH takes any subset and bumps `version`; DELETE → 204). A field outside the question's kind → 422 `invalid` with `details.field`. Over `EVAL_QUESTIONS_PER_AGENT` → 422 `eval_question_limit`. A caller without `can_use` of the agent's workspace → 404.
- C-862 **Scoring** (P4-EVAL, D-289(b)): numeric and text questions get the platform's instruction, appended to the question text, to end the reply with a final line `ANSWER: <value>`; the value is read from the last such line. Numeric: the parsed decimal number within `tolerance` of `expected_number`. Text: trimmed, whitespace collapsed, case-folded equality with `expected_text`. No such line → `fail` with `no_answer_line`. Judged: the judge verdict (C-869). Outcomes `pass` | `fail` | `error` | `not_scored` (C-867). Pass rate = pass ÷ (pass + fail).
- C-863 **Agent eval settings** (P4-EVAL, D-292(b)): `GET /v1/agents/{agent}/eval-settings` (`can_use`) → `{ judge: { secret_id, model_id, secret_label, usable: bool } | null, regression_notify: bool }`. `PUT` (`{ judge: { secret_id, model_id } | null, regression_notify: bool }`) → 200. A change of `judge` needs `can_own` (403 `forbidden` otherwise); the judge secret must be an LLM secret, granted to the agent, usable per readiness (`08 §Model readiness`) and governance-admitted, else 422 `eval_judge_not_granted`, `eval_judge_not_usable` or `eval_judge_not_permitted`. Stored in `agents.eval_judge_secret_id`, `eval_judge_model_id`, `eval_regression_notify`. A judge change is session only. The secret value is never in a response.
- C-864 **Runs** (P4-EVAL): `EvalRun = { id, agent: { id, name }, trigger: "manual"|"schedule", schedule: { id, name }|null, started_by: { id, label }|null, status: "queued"|"running"|"done"|"failed"|"cancelled", error_code: string|null, question_set_version: string, fingerprint_hash: string, counts: { total, passed, failed, errored, not_scored, refused_writes }, pass_rate: number|null, judge_usage: { input_tokens, output_tokens }|null, regression: { from_run_id, drop_points }|null, created_at, started_at|null, finished_at|null }`. `POST /v1/agents/{agent}/eval-runs` (`can_use`; body `{}`) → 202 `EvalRun` (`queued`). Refusals: 409 `eval_run_active` (`EVAL_RUNS_ACTIVE_PER_AGENT`), 429 `eval_runs_limited` (`EVAL_RUNS_PER_AGENT_DAY`, `Retry-After` to the next UTC midnight), 423 `frozen`, 422 `eval_no_questions`. `GET …/eval-runs` (`can_use`): cursor page, filters `status`, `trigger`. `GET /v1/eval-runs/{id}` (`can_use` of the run's workspace; 404 otherwise) → `EvalRun` plus `fingerprint` (the C-866 object) and `results: EvalResult[]` (≤ 100 per page, `?results_after=` cursor, position order). `POST /v1/eval-runs/{id}/cancel` (`can_use`) → 202; a finished run → 409 `eval_run_finished`. `DELETE /v1/eval-runs/{id}` (`can_own`) → 204; a `queued` or `running` run → 409 `eval_run_active`.
- C-865 **Run lifecycle and the due-work arm** (P4-EVAL, D-290(d)): `queued → running → done | failed | cancelled`. The arm claims a run under `lease_until`, renewed while it runs; a lost lease resumes at the next unfinished question with the same `source_event_id`. Before each question the starter's (for a schedule, the creator's under the anchor rule) `can_use` and the agent's freeze are re-checked: a lost right → `cancelled` with `error_code: "access_lost"`; a freeze → `cancelled` with `"frozen"`. Each question: create its `eval` conversation (C-860), one turn with `source_event_id = eval:<run_id>:<position>` and the question text (with the C-862 instruction for numeric and text), wait for the terminal state up to `EVAL_TURN_TIMEOUT` (abort on timeout, reason `timeout`), read the final answer (the durable `text` after the last tool call, `10 §Transcript events`), score it and write the result. `cancel_requested_at` aborts the running turn; the remaining questions are `not_scored` (reason `cancelled`). A question-level failure never fails the run; `failed` is only for a run that cannot proceed at all.
- C-866 **Configuration fingerprint** (P4-EVAL, D-291): `GET /v1/agents/{agent}/eval-fingerprint` (`can_use`) → `{ fingerprint, fingerprint_hash, question_set_version }`, what a run started now would record. `fingerprint` is a canonical JSON object (keys sorted, no insignificant whitespace): `instructions_sha256` (of `agents.brief`), `harness`, `model: { provider, endpoint_id, model_id }` (null where unset), `effort`, `skills: [{ tier, name, content_sha256 }]` (the effective set, sorted by tier then name), `granted_rule_ids` (sorted logical ids of the policy rules granted to the agent), `judge: { secret_id, model_id }|null`, `question_set_version` (SHA-256 over the enabled questions' `id` and `version`, sorted). `fingerprint_hash` is the SHA-256 of the canonical bytes. The object holds no brief or skill content, no question text and no secret value. It is stored on the run at start and never changes.
- C-867 **Results** (P4-EVAL, D-289(c)): `EvalResult = { position, question: { id: string|null, text }, expected_kind, expected: { number?, tolerance?, text?, criteria? } | null (agent-audience: `can_use` only), outcome: "pass"|"fail"|"error"|"not_scored", reason_code: string|null, extracted_value: string|null, answer: string|null (≤ EVAL_ANSWER_CHARS, sanitised; `can_use` only), judge: { verdict, rationale }|null, refused_writes: int, conversation_id: uuid|null, started_at, finished_at }`. `reason_code` vocabulary: `no_answer_line`, `timeout`, `cancelled`, `access_lost`, `frozen`, `judge_not_configured`, `judge_not_granted`, `judge_not_permitted`, `judge_unavailable`, `judge_timeout`, `judge_unparseable`, or a failed turn's `error_code` verbatim.
- C-868 **Side-effect refusal in eval turns** (P4-EVAL, D-290(c), SEC-114): in a turn whose context credential names a conversation of kind `eval`, a gateway request whose effect is a write, and a platform write (`send_message`, `send_file`, `send_as_me`, memory and skill writes, schedule, webhook and peer-task creation), is refused with the standard error body and code `eval_read_only` (403 at the gateway; a tool error with the same code to the agent). No approval row is created; the refusal is counted in `refused_writes`. A read-only app tool (`07 §Read-only connections`) and GET/HEAD requests run as usual.
- C-869 **`PlatformLlm` judge** (P4-EVAL, D-292, SEC-115): the API's internal service, with the purpose `eval_judge` only in phase 5: `complete(purpose, secret_id, model_id, request)`. Checks in order, each a refusal with no request sent: secret `active` and usable (`eval_judge_not_usable`), granted to the run's agent (`eval_judge_not_granted`), admitted by governance for its endpoint and scope (`eval_judge_not_permitted`). Request: one user message `{ question, criteria | expected_text, answer }` with the platform's own judge instructions (never the agent's brief), `max_tokens = EVAL_JUDGE_MAX_TOKENS`, timeout `EVAL_JUDGE_TIMEOUT`, no tools, no streaming. Response parsed as strict JSON `{ "verdict": "pass" | "fail", "rationale": string }` (rationale cut to `EVAL_RATIONALE_CHARS`); anything else → `judge_unparseable`. Transport failure → `judge_unavailable`; timeout → `judge_timeout`. Each failure is an `error` result for that question (C-867); there is no fallback key, provider or retry elsewhere. Usage `{ input_tokens, output_tokens }` is added to the run's `judge_usage`, never to `turns.usage`.
- C-870 **Eval schedules** (P4-EVAL, D-293(a)): `EvalSchedule = { id, agent: { id, name }, name, cron, timezone, enabled, disabled_reason: "user"|"authorization"|"failures"|null, anchor: "creator"|"workspace", creator: { id, label }|null, next_fire_at, last_fire_at, last_outcome: "ok"|"failed"|"skipped_busy"|"skipped_frozen"|null, last_run_id: uuid|null }`. `GET|POST /v1/agents/{agent}/eval-schedules` (`can_use`; POST `{ name, cron, timezone, enabled?, anchor? }`; `anchor` is `can_own` only; a cron below `EVAL_SCHEDULE_MIN_INTERVAL` over a week of occurrences → 422 `invalid`; over `EVAL_SCHEDULES_PER_AGENT` → 422 `eval_schedule_limit`). `PATCH|DELETE /v1/eval-schedules/{id}` (`can_use`; the edit rules of `10 §Schedules`, creator reassignment through the schedules routes). A fire is a due-work claim with `SCHEDULE_FIRE_LEASE` and the fire-time authority of `04 §Fire-time authority`: frozen → `skipped_frozen`; an active run → `skipped_busy`; otherwise a run with `trigger: "schedule"` and no `started_by`. `SCHEDULE_FAILURE_DISABLE` consecutive failed runs disable it (`disabled_reason: "failures"`).
- C-871 **Regression notice** (P4-EVAL, D-293(d)): at finish a `done` run is compared with the previous `done` run of the same `question_set_version`; when its `pass_rate` is at least `EVAL_REGRESSION_DROP` points lower, the run carries `regression: { from_run_id, drop_points }` and the Evals page shows a notice to eligible viewers. The notice carries counts and ids only; nothing is posted to a connector, e-mailed or pushed. Nothing is set when `eval_regression_notify` is false.
- C-872 **Limits and error codes** (P4-EVAL, `02 §Limits`): `EVAL_QUESTIONS_PER_AGENT` (422 `eval_question_limit`), `EVAL_QUESTION_CHARS`, `EVAL_EXPECTED_TEXT_CHARS`, `EVAL_CRITERIA_CHARS` (422 `invalid`), `EVAL_ANSWER_CHARS`, `EVAL_TURN_TIMEOUT`, `EVAL_RUNS_ACTIVE_PER_AGENT` (409 `eval_run_active`), `EVAL_RUNS_PER_AGENT_DAY` (429 `eval_runs_limited`), `EVAL_SCHEDULE_MIN_INTERVAL` and `EVAL_SCHEDULES_PER_AGENT` (422), `EVAL_JUDGE_TIMEOUT`, `EVAL_JUDGE_MAX_TOKENS`, `EVAL_JUDGE_INPUT_MAX`, `EVAL_RATIONALE_CHARS`, `EVAL_RUN_RETENTION` (retention job), `EVAL_REGRESSION_DROP`. Route-level codes: `eval_not_supported` (422), `eval_no_questions` (422), `eval_run_finished` (409), `eval_judge_*` (422), `frozen` (423).
- C-873 **Audit and dashboard** (P4-EVAL, `04 §Audit`, `14`): audit actions `eval.question.create|update|delete`, `eval.settings.update` (critical when the judge changes), `eval.run.start|cancel|delete`, `eval.schedule.create|update|delete`; metadata carries ids and counts (and `fingerprint_hash` on start), never an expected answer, an answer, a rationale or a secret value. Dashboard: **Behaviour › Evals** (`?tab=evals`; hosted agents; `can_use`), with the parts and primitives of `14`.
- C-874 **Tests** (P4-EVAL, `18 §Evals`): per SEC-114..SEC-116 "Proven by"; the questions and settings ITs (P4-EVAL-1), the run engine, refusal and schedule ITs (P4-EVAL-2), the judge ITs with a mock provider (P4-EVAL-3), the dashboard vitest and Playwright (P4-EVAL-4). A live judge check against a real provider needs a granted test key and is not a gate.
### Phase 5 — P5-GUIDE (app guides and tools on demand, D-170..D-172, D-295..D-298, SEC-91, SEC-117..SEC-120)

Rule: `24-app-guides.md` (normative), `09 §2`, `§3.4 agent`, `§6`, `§9`, `§10`, `§12`, `10 §Agent instructions`, `11 §Sandbox image`, `12 §Home projection`, `12 §Platform tools`, `16 §W5`, `03` (`plugin_blobs`), `05` (plugin routes), `14` (Plugins), `15` (`hosted.platform_tools`), `02 §Limits`, `17` (SEC-91, SEC-117..SEC-120), `18 §App guides on demand`. Reserved numbers C-640..C-659 (C-651..C-657 used by the 2026-11-15 amendment; C-658, C-659 free). **Amendment of 2026-11-15 (D-295..D-297)** changes C-640, C-641, C-643, C-645, C-646, C-648, C-649 and C-650 in place (marked "D-295" / "D-296") and adds C-651..C-657. Everything is additive: a package, a snapshot, a home sync and a `PluginPackage` that do not use the feature are byte-identical to before.

- C-640 **Manifest and archive** (P5-GUIDE-1). `apps[].agent` gains `summary?: string` (one line, ≤ `AGENT_SUMMARY_MAX`) and `guide?: [{ file, title }]` (≤ `AGENT_GUIDE_FILES_MAX`; `file` = `[a-z0-9][a-z0-9_-]{0,47}\.(md|txt|json|yaml|tsv|csv)`, not `guide.md` / `tools.md`; `title` one line, 1–120 chars). Archive path `agent/<app_id>/<file>`: listed ⇔ present, UTF-8 without control characters other than LF and TAB and without BOM, ≤ `AGENT_GUIDE_FILE_MAX` per file and ≤ `AGENT_GUIDE_APP_MAX` per app. Violation codes: `agent_summary_too_long`, `agent_summary_multiline`, `agent_summary_required`, `agent_guide_file_name`, `agent_guide_reserved_name`, `agent_guide_duplicate_file`, `agent_guide_title`, `agent_guide_unlisted_file`, `agent_guide_missing_file`, `agent_guide_unknown_app`, `agent_guide_not_text`, `agent_guide_too_many_files`, `agent_guide_file_too_large`, `agent_guide_app_too_large`, `agent_guide_computed_field` (`sha256` / `size` in a submitted guide entry: they exist only in the snapshot). (D-295) A guide entry is `{ file, title, kind? }` with `kind` ∈ `reference` (default) | `recipe`; a recipe is `.md` (`agent_recipe_not_markdown`); an unknown `kind` is `agent_guide_kind`. `api.json` joins the reserved names. (D-296) `agent.operations?: string` names one `.json` file in `agent/<app_id>/` that is not in `guide` (C-651); an archive file under `agent/<app_id>/` is valid when listed in `guide` or named by `operations`; `summary` is required when `guide` or `operations` is present. (D-296, P5-GUIDE-6) The app id `aurora` is reserved for the platform target of `aurora-agent`: a package with an app of that id is rejected (`app_id_reserved`), whether or not it uses the agent guide.
- C-641 **Snapshot** (P5-GUIDE-1). `apps[].agent.summary?: string`, `apps[].agent.guide?: [{ file, title, kind: "reference"|"recipe", sha256: hex, size: u64 }]` (`kind` per D-295, always present in the snapshot), (D-296) `apps[].agent.operations?: { file, sha256: hex, size: u64, count: u32 }`, each omitted when absent; `format` and `contract` unchanged. No file content in the snapshot.
- C-642 **Storage** (P5-GUIDE-1). Migration `<date>_agent_guide_blobs.sql`: the `plugin_blobs.kind` check gains `agent_guide`. Each guide file of a non-`invalid` package is one blob keyed by the sha256 of its bytes, written in the ingest transaction; readers of guide files select **by digest only** (a row of another kind with the same bytes is the same content). The stored `plugin_packages.manifest` is the submitted manifest, unchanged.
- C-643 **Home sync part `apps`** (P5-GUIDE-2). `HomeSyncKind` gains `apps` (sent after `tools`). Entries `{ path: ".aurora/apps/<app_id>/<file>", content: string }`; per app in the render inputs: `guide.md`, `tools.md`, (D-296) `api.json` (C-652, except for database apps), then the package's guide files in manifest order (`.md` / `.txt` with the one-line third-party header of `24 §5.3` — *Procedure* for a recipe, D-295 — other extensions verbatim; the operations file itself is not projected). Every path is in the `manifest` part. Reference files stop at `AGENT_GUIDE_SYNC_MAX` per sync in `app_id` order (whole apps; generated files always sent; metric `aurora_home_guide_budget_exceeded_total`). Supervisor: `.aurora/apps/` is a managed root; an entry is accepted only when its path is exactly `.aurora/apps/<id>/<name>` (nothing deeper) with `<id>` matching the id grammar of `09 §3` and `<name>` matching the file grammar of C-640 or equal to `guide.md` / `tools.md`; mode 0444, directories 0555; pruned to the manifest at commit.
- C-644 **Render inputs** (P5-GUIDE-2). `render_inputs.apps[]` = `{ name, accounts, docs_links, snippet, app_id: string, summary: string|null, guide_path: string }` (the last three new; `summary` = `agent.summary`, else `about.description`, sanitized to one line and clamped to `AGENT_SUMMARY_MAX`; `guide_path` = `.aurora/apps/<app_id>/guide.md`). `accounts[]` is unchanged; the host of an account (the instance host of an instance-bound connection, the app's `default_host` when the connection has no instance, the stored `tenant_host` of a tenant-bound one) is resolved by the API and printed in `guide.md` only (C-646).
- C-645 **Instruction file** (P5-GUIDE-2; **amended by D-295**). *Connected apps* is **always** the **index** of `24 §6` (one fixed paragraph, then per app ``- <name> (`<app_id>`, <n> account(s)): <summary>``); the paragraph's `aurora-agent` sentence is rendered only with `agent_cli` (C-653). It falls back to today's inline text only when an app entry lacks `guide_path` (an older API). `AGENT_NOTES_INLINE_MAX` does not exist. The preamble gains the *working with app data* paragraph of `24 §6` when `apps` is not empty (its last sentence only with `agent_cli`). Texts are pinned by `insta` snapshots in `services/aurora-supervisor/src/instructions.rs`.
- C-646 **`guide.md`** (P5-GUIDE-1 renderer). Sections in order, empty ones omitted: title `# <name> (<app_id>)` and the provenance line; **Accounts** (the `x-aurora-connection-id` rule; per account `` `<id>`: <label> `` + ` (host <host>)` or ``(database: `psql service=<name>`)``); **Your access** (per account `full access` or `custom access: <a> allowed, <b> need approval, <c> blocked`; pointer to `tools.md`); (D-295) **Recipes** (fixed lead sentence, then `<file>`: `<title>` per recipe in manifest order); **Files in this folder** (`tools.md`, (D-296) `api.json` with the `aurora-agent` line when C-652 is rendered, then `<file>`: `<title>` per reference file, or the budget notice); **Documentation** (`api_docs`, `api_spec`); **Notes from the <name> plugin (third-party)** with the essentials in a fence longer than any backtick run they contain. Untrusted strings pass the one-line sanitizer with the clamps of the instruction renderer.
- C-647 **`tools.md`** (P5-GUIDE-1 renderer). Header and provenance paragraph of `24 §5.2`; **Accounts** lettered `A`, `B`, … in render-input order with the grant mode; **Read** and **Write** with one line per catalogue item in catalogue order: `` - `<tool id>` <METHODS> [<host> ]<path>[ (<classifier>)]: <name>.[ <description>.] [<access>] ``; **Requests that match no operation above**. `<access>` ∈ `allowed` | `approval` | `blocked` | `unavailable`, printed once when equal for every account, else `A: …; B: …`. Derivation from the grant row: `full` → `allowed`; `custom` → `allowed` if the id is in `allow_tools`, `approval` if in `ask_tools`, else `blocked`; a tool on a host entry with `claim_status: lost` → `unavailable`; the last section is `allowed` only for `full` on an app without `grants.block_untooled`. Group wildcards are not listed.
- C-648 **Admin API** (P5-GUIDE-2; consumed by P5-GUIDE-3). `PluginPackage` gains `agent_guides: [{ app_id, summary: string|null, instructions: string, files: [{ file, title, kind: "reference"|"recipe", size, sha256 }], operations: null | { file, size, sha256, count }, changed: null | { summary: bool, instructions: bool, operations: bool, files: [{ file, change: "added"|"changed"|"removed" }] } }]` (`kind`, `operations` per D-295 / D-296; the file route also serves the operations file) (one entry per app, in manifest order; `changed` is `null` when the package has no active version or this is it). `GET /v1/admin/plugins/{id}/agent-guides/{app_id}/{file}` (instance admin; 404 `not_found` otherwise shaped) → the file bytes, `Content-Type: text/plain; charset=utf-8`, `X-Content-Type-Options: nosniff`, `Content-Disposition: attachment; filename="<file>"`, `Cache-Control: private, no-store`. Audit: `plugin.approve` and `plugin.activate` metadata gain `agent_guide_changed: [app_id]` (omitted when empty). OpenAPI regenerated.
- C-649 **CLI** (P5-GUIDE-1). `aurora plugin guide [--app <id>] [--access full|custom] [--out <dir>]`: renders C-646 / C-647, (D-296) C-652 when the app has operations or tools, the recipes and the reference files for one sample account (`custom`: read tools `allowed`, write tools `approval`, nothing else) with `aurora-registry::agent_guide::render`; without `--out`, to stdout with a `==> <path> <==` line per file. `new` scaffolds `agent.summary`, `agent.instructions`, `agent/<app_id>/api.md` and its `guide` entry; `build` packs `agent/**`; `docs` includes the agent guide.
- C-650 **OpenAPI import** (P5-GUIDE-5). `aurora plugin import-openapi <file> --app <id> [--host <host>] [--dir <package dir>]`: input OpenAPI 3.0 / 3.1, JSON or YAML, local file, local `$ref`s only (an external `$ref` is an error); output `agent/<app_id>/operations.md` (per tag, `- METHOD path: summary [operationId]`), `agent/<app_id>/ops-<tag>.md` (per operation: parameters `name (in, required?, type)`, top-level JSON body fields), the matching `guide` entries printed for the manifest, and a `tools:` skeleton on stdout (`read` = `GET`/`HEAD`, `write` = other methods; id from `operationId`, else from method and path; `{param}` → `*`). Byte-stable across runs (sorted by tag, path, method). Over `AGENT_GUIDE_APP_MAX` it drops body fields, then parameter descriptions, then the per-tag files of the largest tags, and reports each drop on stderr; exit status 0. It writes nothing outside `agent/<app_id>/`. (D-296) Also writes `operations.json` (C-656).
- C-651 **Operations file** (P5-GUIDE-6, D-296). `agent/<app_id>/<file>.json` named by `agent.operations`; JSON object with exactly `format: "aurora.operations/1"` and `operations: [Operation]` (1..`AGENT_OPERATIONS_MAX`); `Operation` = `{ id, method, path, host?, summary, tags?, params?, body?, pagination?, items?, tool? }` with the grammars and rules of `24 §3.5` (closed schema, unknown keys rejected). `Param` = `{ name, in: "path"|"query"|"header", required?: bool (default false; `path` params are required), type: "string"|"integer"|"number"|"boolean"|"array", description?, enum?, default? }`; `body` = `null | { content_type: "application/json"|"application/x-www-form-urlencoded"|"multipart/form-data", fields?: [Param without in] }`; `pagination` = `null | { style: "link_header" } | { style: "page_param", param, start?: 0|1, size_param? } | { style: "cursor", param, next: JsonPath }`. Size ≤ `AGENT_OPERATIONS_FILE_MAX`, UTF-8 text as C-640. Violation codes: `agent_operations_format`, `agent_operations_json`, `agent_operations_unknown_key`, `agent_operations_id`, `agent_operations_duplicate_id`, `agent_operations_method`, `agent_operations_path`, `agent_operations_path_param`, `agent_operations_host`, `agent_operations_param`, `agent_operations_header`, `agent_operations_body`, `agent_operations_pagination`, `agent_operations_json_path`, `agent_operations_tool`, `agent_operations_too_many`, `agent_operations_too_large`, `agent_operations_listed` (the file also appears in `guide`).
- C-652 **`api.json`** (P5-GUIDE-6, D-296). Rendered by `aurora-registry::agent_guide::render` per agent and app, projected at `.aurora/apps/<app_id>/api.json` in the `apps` part (C-643). `{ format: "aurora.app-api/1", app: { id, name, version }, accounts: [{ letter, connection_id, label, base_url, grant: "full"|"custom" }], operations: [{ id, method, path, host: string|null, summary, tags, params, body, pagination, items: string|null, tool: string|null, access: { <letter>: "allowed"|"approval"|"blocked"|"unavailable" }, source: "package"|"catalogue" }], recipes: [{ file, title }], untooled: { <letter>: state }, truncated?: true }`; keys in this order, deterministic; ≤ `AGENT_API_JSON_MAX` with the drop order of `24 §5.4`. `tool` for a package operation without one = the catalogue tool the gateway's matcher selects for (method, host, path with `{param}` → a placeholder segment); access per C-647's derivation. Not rendered for database apps.
- C-653 **`aurora-agent`** (P5-GUIDE-6, D-296; one command for apps and platform tools, owner 2026-11-15). Subcommand `aurora-supervisor agent` with the verbs `list [<target>]`, `search <words…> [--in <target>] [--limit N]`, `describe <target> <name>`, `call <target> <name> …` and the options, output and exit codes of `24 §10a`; a target is a connected app id or `aurora` (C-655). Exit codes: 0 success, 1 upstream HTTP error or tool error result, 2 usage / validation / an option that does not apply to the target / no session for `aurora`, 3 Aurora gateway refusal, 4 tools-socket transport failure. App targets: `--account`, `--all` up to `AGENT_CLI_PAGES_DEFAULT` / `AGENT_CLI_PAGES_MAX` pages, `--path` / `--method`, ≤ `AGENT_CLI_SAVE_MAX` bytes saved. Outputs under `/tmp/aurora-agent/` (0700) unless `--out` (refused under `/workspace/.aurora/` and `/workspace/memory/`); previews ≤ `AGENT_CLI_PREVIEW_MAX`. Image: one wrapper `/opt/aurora/bin/aurora-agent` (`exec /usr/local/bin/aurora-supervisor agent "$@"`) in the OS layer (`11 §Sandbox image`). **`agent_cli`** = the supervisor has the subcommand (always true from this release) **and** the wrapper exists and is executable at supervisor start; it is a supervisor-local fact, never a render input, and gates the `aurora-agent` sentences of C-645 and the `on_demand` behaviour of C-654. Until P5-GUIDE-7, target `aurora` answers "not available in this version" (exit 2) and is omitted from `list` and `search`.
- C-654 **Platform tool exposure** (P5-GUIDE-7, D-297). `PlatformTool` gains `fn exposure(&self) -> Exposure { OnDemand }` and `fn family(&self) -> &'static str`; `ToolDescriptor` (`aurora-protocol::tools`) gains `family?: string` and `exposure?: "core"|"on_demand"` (omitted = `on_demand`; an older API sends neither, and then the supervisor lists every tool). Core set: `send_file`, `complete_task`; at most `PLATFORM_TOOLS_CORE_MAX`. Render inputs gain `platform_tools: { mode: "on_demand"|"all", families: [{ name, purpose, tools: [string] }] }` (available tools only, registry order; absent = `all`). Bridge: `tools/list` returns descriptors with `exposure == "core"` when `mode == "on_demand"` and `agent_cli`, else all; `tools/call` forwards any name as before. Socket `list` is unchanged and returns all available tools. Instruction file: the *platform tools* fragment of `24 §10b` (fixed paragraph, then per family `- <name> (<verbs>): <purpose>`, verbs = tool names with the family prefix stripped) when `mode == "on_demand"` and `agent_cli`; nothing otherwise. Idle-session release on catalogue change is computed over the core descriptors plus the rendered instruction files, not over all descriptors.
- C-655 **Target `aurora` of `aurora-agent`** (P5-GUIDE-7, D-297). `list aurora` (families and tools), `search` over tool names, families and descriptions (core tools included), `describe aurora <tool>` (description and input schema as a parameter table plus an example), `call aurora <tool> [k=v | k:=json …] [--body @file|-] [--raw]` (`24 §10a`, *Target `aurora`*); talks to `/run/aurora/tools.sock` with the existing `list` / `call` requests (`12 §Path`), `session` = `$AURORA_SESSION`; `--account`, `--all`, `--max-pages`, `--path`, `--method` refused (exit 2). The supervisor sets `AURORA_SESSION=<conversation id>` in the environment of every harness process it starts (both adapters); it is not set for the web terminal, SSH sessions or `process_start` children. A result over `AGENT_CLI_PREVIEW_MAX` is saved as `/tmp/aurora-agent/aurora-<tool>-<UTC timestamp>.json` with a preview printed.
- C-656 **OpenAPI import, operations** (P5-GUIDE-6, D-296; extends C-650). `import-openapi` additionally writes `agent/<app_id>/operations.json` (C-651) and prints the `agent.operations: operations.json` line for the manifest: one operation per OpenAPI operation, `id` = the tool id of the skeleton, `tool` = that id, `params` from path/query/header parameters (forbidden headers dropped with a stderr note; cookie parameters dropped), `body` from a JSON / form request body's top-level properties, `items` = `$` for an array response or `$.<prop>` when the 200 response object has exactly one array property, `pagination` = `link_header` when the 200 response declares a `Link` header, `page_param` when a query parameter is named `page`; sorted as C-650; over `AGENT_OPERATIONS_FILE_MAX` it drops descriptions, then body fields, then `params` of the largest tags (reported on stderr).
- C-657 **Configuration** (P5-GUIDE-7, D-297). API key `hosted.platform_tools`: `"on_demand"` (default) | `"all"`; an unknown value refuses startup; copied into the render inputs (C-654). Helm: `config.hosted.platform_tools` (passed through, `23`). An instance setting in the `agents` section (`15 §Instance settings`, SET-5), read at use; whichever of SET-5 and P5-GUIDE-7 merges second adds it to that section.

### Phase 5 — P5-MODELS (the harness chooses models: roles, subagent default, usage per model, D-302..D-304, SEC-122, SEC-123)

Rule: `08 §Model selection`, `§Model roles`, `§Model readiness`, *Error classification and usage*, `§11.5`; `10 §Agents`, `§Agent instructions`, `§Usage and metering`, `§Evals`; `12 §Harness interface`, `§Adapter claude-code`, `§Adapter codex`; `16` (`ModelChoice`); `03` (`agents`, `turns.usage`, `request_log`); `05` (agent route, `/v1/harnesses`, readiness); `15` (`llm.endpoints[]`); `14` (Instructions, Access › Model readiness, Admin › LLM endpoints, Activity); `02 §Limits`; `17` (SEC-122, SEC-123); `18 §Model roles and usage per model`. Reserved numbers C-875..C-889 (C-875..C-884 used). Everything is additive: an agent and an endpoint without roles, and a harness without a per-model report, behave exactly as before, except that `claude-code` usage now includes subagent tokens.

- C-875 **Endpoint entry keys** (P5-MODELS-1, D-303; extends C-800). Every provider's entry accepts `model_roles: { large?: string, medium?: string, small?: string }` and `model_info: { [model_id]: { context_window?: u32 (1 000..10 000 000), capabilities?: ("effort"|"thinking"|"adaptive_thinking"|"interleaved_thinking")[] } }` (≤ `MODEL_INFO_MAX`). It accepts them in TOML and as the same JSON in the settings value `llm.endpoints` (C-779). Ids match `MODEL_ID_PATTERN` = `^[A-Za-z0-9._:/@-]{1,200}$`. A role must be on the entry's `allowed_models` when that is set, and on `models` when the list is static. Anything else refuses startup or the settings write with `value_invalid` and `details: { key }` (D-248(c)). The `llm` settings section returns both keys like the rest of the entry.
- C-876 **Agent fields** (P5-MODELS-1; `GET`/`PATCH /v1/agents/{agent}`, hosted agents). The response gains `model_roles: { endpoint_id: string, large: string|null, medium: string|null, small: string|null } | null` and `subagent_model: "inherit"|"large"|"medium"|"small"` (default `"inherit"`). In a PATCH, `model_roles` is either null, which clears it, or an object with `endpoint_id` and any subset of the roles, which replaces the object. A role that is null or absent is unset. The permission is that of `model`. Errors: 422 `model_id_invalid` `{ field }`; 422 `llm_model_not_allowed` `{ field, model, allowed_models }`; 422 `model_role_endpoint_mismatch` `{ endpoint_id, default_endpoint_id }` when `endpoint_id` is not the endpoint of the agent's current default selection (C-500 `default.endpoint_id`). Changing either field is audited with the agent's other settings and is a render-input change (session released while idle).
- C-877 **`ModelChoice` additions and `claude-code` mapping** (P5-MODELS-1; `16`). `roles?: { large?: string, medium?: string, small?: string }` holds only resolved roles; `subagent_role?: "large"|"medium"|"small"`; `model_meta?: [{ model: string, context_window?: u32, capabilities?: string[] }]` covers the default and resolved roles only. The `claude-code` environment:
  - `ANTHROPIC_DEFAULT_OPUS_MODEL` ← `roles.large`, `ANTHROPIC_DEFAULT_SONNET_MODEL` ← `roles.medium`, `ANTHROPIC_DEFAULT_HAIKU_MODEL` ← `roles.small`;
  - `CLAUDE_CODE_SUBAGENT_MODEL` ← `opus`|`sonnet`|`haiku` for `subagent_role`;
  - `ANTHROPIC_DEFAULT_{OPUS,SONNET,HAIKU}_MODEL_SUPPORTED_CAPABILITIES` ← comma-joined `capabilities` of that role's model;
  - `CLAUDE_CODE_MAX_CONTEXT_TOKENS` ← the default model's `context_window`.

  The names are pinned with the CLI version and verified by the live suite. A per-alias context variable is used only if the pinned version has one. The supervisor re-validates every value (SEC-122). `HarnessCapabilities.model_roles`: `claude-code` declares `["large","medium","small"]`, while `codex` and `fake` declare `[]`.
- C-878 **Readiness** (P5-MODELS-1; extends C-500). `default` (when non-null) gains `roles: { large: RoleView, medium: RoleView, small: RoleView }`, `RoleView = { model_id: string|null, source: "agent"|"endpoint"|"none", warning: null|"role_unset"|"role_not_allowed" }`. It also gains `roles_dropped: string[]` (roles dropped for the next turn), `subagent_model` and `subagent_warning: null|"subagent_role_unset"`. Same function as the dispatcher.
- C-879 **Usage per model** (P5-MODELS-2, D-304). `HarnessEvent::Done { usage }` and the `usage` of `turn.result` (`16`) gain `by_model: [{ model, input_tokens, output_tokens, cache_read_tokens, cache_write_tokens }]`. The API stores `turns.usage` = totals (the sum of the kept entries) + `provider`, `endpoint_id` (from `ModelChoice`) + `model` (the default) + `by_model` + `unverified_models?: true`. The trust rule of `08` applies: the allowed set is the default, the resolved roles and the effective allow-list (or any pattern-valid id when no allow-list applies), and the rest is folded into the default's entry, with at most `USAGE_BY_MODEL_MAX` entries. Turn views that already return `usage` return the new fields. The workspace usage route's `by_model` (D-260(g)) sums `by_model` entries per `(provider, endpoint_id, model)`, and a turn without `by_model` counts under `model`.
- C-880 **`request_log.llm_model`** (P5-MODELS-2). It is a new nullable column on the partitioned parent, inherited by every partition. The migration takes the next free number when placed. The gateway fills it for every request with an injected key on a governed LLM endpoint, using `model_from_request` (`Some(id)`; null for `None`/`Unknown`; never a refusal without an allow-list). Request-log rows in `GET /v1/workspaces/{ws}/activity` and the other request-log reads gain `llm_model: string|null`.
- C-881 **Terminal models** (P5-MODELS-1; amends C-750 (4)). `TerminalModel.choice` carries the C-877 additions. The `claude-code` section of `harness.env` sets the same variables as a turn, with every value single-quoted (SEC-122).
- C-882 **`GET /v1/harnesses`** (P5-MODELS-1; extends C-420). `HarnessView` gains `model_roles: string[]`.
- C-883 **Eval fingerprint** (P5-MODELS-1; amends C-866). It gains `model_roles: { large: string|null, medium: string|null, small: string|null }` (resolved) and `subagent_model`, and the field-level diff shows them.
- C-884 **Instructions fragment** (P5-MODELS-1; `10 §Agent instructions`). It is a fixed template, rendered only when a role resolves or `subagent_role` is set. Its ids are inserted verbatim: they are pattern-checked, so they need no escaping beyond Markdown code spans.

### FIX-152 (failed re-authentication returns to its page; `auth_time` evidence, D-305)

- C-890 **Re-authentication failure redirect and audit detail** (FIX-152, D-305; amends the `/v1/auth/oidc/{provider}/callback` row of `05`).
  - **Redirect.** Once the callback has opened the flow state, every failure of a re-authentication flow (`reauth=1`) redirects 302 to the flow's `return_to` with `reauth_error=<code>`. The code goes before any `#fragment`, after `&` if the path has a query and `?` otherwise; codes are fixed snake_case and not encoded. Failures before the state opens, and every failure of a normal sign-in, still go to `/auth/login?error=<code>`.
  - **Audit.** A `reauth_stale` denial (`user.login`, `denied`) gains exactly one of `auth_time_age_s: i64` (`now − auth_time`; negative means in the future) or `auth_time_missing: true`. A `warn` log carries the provider id and the same value, with no identity data.
  - **Dashboard.** `/account/delete`, `/admin/orgs`, `/admin/orgs/[org]` and `/o/[org]/settings/directory` show a danger `Notice` for `reauth_error`, with text from the error-code map and a generic fallback for unknown codes.

### FIX-153 / FIX-154 (connecting and granting apps without dead ends; credential-free apps in one step, D-306)

Rule: `90 D-306`, `06 §LDAP lane` and §Error codes, `05` (apps and grants rows), `14 §Visual design`. Reserved C-891..C-897. No migration. D-251(d) and SEC-110 unchanged.

- C-891 **Gateway dashboard links** (FIX-153; amends the `connect_url`, `reconnect_url`, `create_secret_url` details of `06 §Error codes`). One builder in the gateway produces every dashboard URL it emits, from `external_url`, the org and workspace slugs of the resolved host:
  - `connect_url(app)` = `/o/<org>/w/<ws>/credentials?tab=apps&connect=<app>` (the C-842 deep link); with no app, `…/credentials?tab=apps`.
  - `reconnect_url(conn)` = `/o/<org>/w/<ws>/credentials?tab=connections&reconnect=<connection id>`.
  - `create_secret_url` = `/o/<org>/w/<ws>/credentials?tab=secrets`.
  - `access_url(agent)` = `/o/<org>/w/<ws>/agents/<agent id>?tab=access` (C-894).
  - `manage_url` (`…/policy`, `/o/<org>/policy`, `/o/<org>/governance`) unchanged.
  - The paths `/o/<org>/w/<ws>/apps/<app>` and `/o/<org>/w/<ws>/secrets` are no longer emitted (no dashboard page serves them). A shared fixture lists every template; the gateway's unit test pins its output to the fixture and a web test checks that each template is an existing route and that each `tab` value is one the page accepts.
- C-892 **LDAP lane refusals** (FIX-153; amends C-761). A request on `<app id>.ldap.aurora.internal` that reaches the lane check without the approved block is answered:
  - plain HTTP (not TLS through the proxy) → `400 ldap_https_required`, `details: { app_id?, url: "https://<host>/search" }`, message naming the HTTPS URL;
  - an installed LDAP app with no connection in scope → `403 ldap_no_connection`, `details: { app_id, app_name, reason: "not_connected", connect_url, access_url }`; message "<App name> is not connected in this workspace. A workspace owner can connect it at <connect_url> and give this agent access.";
  - a connection of the app in scope, none granted to this agent → `403 ldap_no_connection`, `details: { app_id, app_name, reason: "not_granted", access_url }`; message "This agent has not been given access to <App name>. A workspace owner can give it on the agent's Access tab: <access_url>";
  - a host of no installed LDAP app → `403 ldap_no_connection`, `details: { reason: "no_app" }`, no links.
  - In every case no directory is dialled (unchanged). The reason is derived from the candidate classification `decide` already computes (`Fallback`), not from a second lookup. The request-log row records the code as before.
- C-893 **`access_restricted` and `app_not_connected` point at the fix** (FIX-153). `access_restricted` for an ungranted candidate gains `details.access_url` and its message names it ("…Ask a workspace owner to give this agent access: <access_url>"); `manage_url` stays in details. The `disabled` variant is unchanged. `app_not_connected` keeps `connect_url` (now C-891's) and its message adds "…then give this agent access".
- C-894 **Dashboard routes** (FIX-153; no wire change; amends C-506).
  - The agent page `/o/<org>/w/<ws>/agents/<segment>` accepts an agent id as well as a handle. An id that names an agent of the workspace is replaced (`router.replace`, query kept) by its handle; an unknown id or handle is the existing not-found state.
  - The org Apps page `/o/<org>/apps` lists the installed apps (`GET /v1/apps`), whatever the posture, each with **Connect…**. It opens a dialog to choose one of the caller's workspaces where they are owner. Each workspace is shown as available or not for that app, from `GET /v1/apps?workspace_id=`. Choosing navigates to C-842's deep link. With no owned workspace the dialog says who can connect instead. The posture notice and the restricted-posture allow-list stay on the page.
  - The org Credentials › Connections empty state links to `/o/<org>/apps` (no Apps tab exists on org pages); the workspace one links its "Apps tab" to `?tab=apps`.
- C-895 **`credential_free`** (FIX-154). `AppSummary` (`GET /v1/apps`) and `App` (`GET /v1/apps/{app}`) gain `credential_free: boolean`. True iff the app declares no credentials, no `token`, no `instances`, no `secret_link` and no `database`, and it has at least one connect method and every connect method is `kind: "fields"` with `fields: []` and no scopes. It is derived from the installed manifest (one shared function used by the API and the gateway), not stored.
- C-896 **`PUT /v1/agents/{agent}/grants/apps/{app}`** (FIX-154; S/K, same caller rule as `PUT /v1/agents/{agent}/grants/connections/{conn}`).
  - **Body.** As that route: `{ mode, allow_tools?, ask_tools?, ask_timeout_secs? }`, same validation (incl. 422 `full_grant_forbidden` for `custom_only`).
  - **Checks, before any write.** 404 `app_not_found` when not installed. 403 `app_unavailable` when the posture does not make it available to the agent's workspace. 422 `app_not_credential_free` (`details: { app_id }`) when C-895 is false.
  - **Effect, in one transaction serialized per (workspace, app)** (e.g. `pg_advisory_xact_lock` on the pair), so parallel calls never create two connections:
    1. Take the oldest `active` workspace-scope connection of the app in the agent's workspace. If there is none, create one through the same service path as `POST /v1/apps/{app}/connect` with the first credential-free method. Its label is `connect_note.label_hint`, else the app name. It is audited `connection.created` with `details.via = "agent_grant"` and `details.agent_id`.
    2. Upsert the agent's grant on that connection exactly as `PUT …/grants/connections/{conn}` does, with the same audit, snapshot invalidation and `app_restrictions`.
  - **Answer.** `200 { connection: Connection, grant: ConnectionGrant, created: boolean }`.
  - **Removing access.** Uses the existing `DELETE /v1/agents/{agent}/grants/connections/{conn}`; the connection is kept.
- C-897 **Credential-free apps in the dashboard and the gateway** (FIX-154).
  - **Access tab.** The agent's Access tab (Connections section) also lists each available credential-free app that has no connection in the agent's scope: app icon and name, a "No sign-in needed" badge and **Give access**. It opens the same grant editor (`GrantForm`), which saves through C-896. Once granted, the row becomes the ordinary connection row.
  - **Connect dialog.** For a credential-free app the Connect dialog (C-842) shows the app's `connect_note` and the label, with no form step. It goes straight to the agent picker (`GiveAccessPanel`) with one primary action that connects and then grants (`POST /v1/connections/{id}/grants`).
  - **Gateway.** For a credential-free app, `ldap_no_connection` `not_connected` and `app_not_connected` name `access_url` first ("A workspace owner can give this agent access on its Access tab: <access_url>"); `connect_url` stays in details.
  - **Agent notice.** The agent's `request_app_connection` notice (I-29) for a credential-free app links to the Access tab of the requesting agent instead of the connect deep link.
